# Review of the plan after the first robot test: complete results

Version 1 of `2026-11-11_plan_after_first_robot_test.md` (saved 20:47 on 11.11.26) was read by four critics, each
with one question. A second agent then tested every objection against the files (verdict `stands`, `partly` or
`does not stand`) and wrote the fix it would put into the plan. This file holds all of it, unedited. Version 2 of
the plan was written from the checked fixes. Scripts and outputs: one folder per critic next to this file, the
checker's in `check/`. Nothing was run on the robot, nothing was trained, no git change was made.

## Does the plan say what the verified check says? (folder `evidence`)

**The critic's verdict.** Partly sound, not yet ready to act on. Most numbers in sections 1, 2, 3 and 8 match the check, and the plan took over the checkers' best tests (a shorter fixed step as control, a model that never saw the sheet, solid against dashed prints, a ruler check). But I found one wrong count (170 of 210; the files say 149), about ten sentences that are firmer than their verdicts ('five causes in this order of weight', 'ruled out [measured]', 'all four models behave alike', 'the evidence says matters most'), and verified points that are left out and would change session 2. The three changes that matter most: (1) The driver's step rule is decided on sheet 03 with model E alone; the checkers asked for the loop of sheet 05, the stop place and an unseen line inside that decision, with a rule written first (P1). (2) The picture measure cannot yet be the judge at bends and loops: on the logs of 11.11 the plan's own block A rule fails run 10, the run that passed the bend, and its 'lost' rule marks three finished runs as lost [measured] (P2). (3) Sheet 04 and the stop on an unseen line have no test and no remedy, although two checkers ask for a sheet-04 repeat before any conclusion, and the unseen stop is right in 67 to 80 % of episodes on sheets 01, 03, 04 but in only 21 to 32 % on sheets 02, 05, 06 [measured] (P3, P5). After that, the demonstration rules of step 3 must be squared with the 209 old recordings and with what the arm can do (P4). Where things are: 'CHECK' below means D:/AI_Project/.claude/worktrees/training-analysis/06_deliverables/research_notes/Model and training/evidence/robot_test_2026-11-11/check_results_complete.md . Every file named eNN_... below is in D:/AI_Project/.claude/worktrees/training-analysis/06_deliverables/research_notes/Model and training/evidence/robot_test_2026-11-11/plan_review/evidence/ (scripts, printed outputs, three picture sheets; e01 to e03 are the earlier attempt's: I reran e01 and e02 and checked e03 against the logged twists, difference 0.0000, and against the twist checker's own numbers). Nothing connected to the robot, nothing was trained, no git change, no existing file edited.

**The checker's overall verdict.** Almost all of the critique stands. For every one of the 15 objections I found the evidence the critic names: I read the verdict, challenge and missed lists of all eight sections of CHECK (check_results_complete.md), the plan, the earlier report (sections 7.5, 8.1 to 8.5, 10.1), config.py, model_mode.py and requests_to_professor.md; I reran seven of the critic's scripts (e01, e05, e07, e08, e09, e11, e12: outputs identical, kept in check/reruns/), looked at its picture sheets, read the ten summary.json files, and recomputed three things with my own code. No objection falls. Ten stand as written (P2, P3, P6, P7, P9, P11, P12, P13, P14, P15). Five are right in direction but overstated in one part or carry a fix that must be changed (P1, P4, P5, P8, P10). The ones that should change the plan most: P1 (the driver's step rule is decided without a loop, without the stop place and without a written rule on an unseen line), P2 (the picture measure cannot yet judge at bends: in pictures 4 and 5 of run 10 the puck sits on the ink [my own eyes] where the measure reads 19 and 15 mm [measured in the analyst's table]), P5 (nine of the ten runs were ended by the model's own stop, five of them with the puck off the line [measured, summary.json and steps.csv]) and P3 (sheet 04 has no run). My own checks sharpen three points [calculated: the models are given the recorded or logged pictures, inference only]. (1) On the recorded pictures of sheet 04's first S-bend model E asks for 0.91 to 1.09 of the recorded turn (73.5 of 75.4 degrees over 16 pictures); in run 6 it asked 6.8 degrees. So 'the model asked for 0.29 of the recorded twist' describes the state run 6 was in, not model E on sheet 04 (c03). (2) At the first bend of sheet 03 every one of the team's five members points further into the bend than model E and asks for more twist on the same logged pictures (c02); of the models that never saw sheet 03 the other recipe asks for 0.79 of the recorded turn against 0.66 for E's recipe (c05). (3) The critic's 'median of three pictures' does not rescue run 10 (c06). Weak sides of the critique: taken together its fixes add about 50 full runs and 16 short runs to a session planned at 24 full and 12 short, which is about three sessions instead of one [calculated from the plan's and the earlier report's times per run], and it gives no order; several of its decision rules cannot be read from three or four runs (P1 'the start was the cause', P8 'round 2 starts smaller', P10's go or no-go after four shapes); a pencil line round the puck would mark sheets that are used again; and its corrected sentence 'four reached the line's end and stopped there' repeats an overstatement, because by the 12 mm rule only two of the ten runs ended on the line's end [measured by the checker]. My versions of the fixes fit three sessions of about two and a half hours [guess from run times]: 2a driver settings, the measure and sheet 04; 2b the exam case, the driver decision and the stop; 2c dashes and the start. Each needs its written checklist and goes to Emranul as one decision. Nothing connected to the robot, nothing was trained, no git change, no existing file edited. My scripts and outputs (c01 to c06, reruns/) are in D:/AI_Project/.claude/worktrees/training-analysis/06_deliverables/research_notes/Model and training/evidence/robot_test_2026-11-11/plan_review/evidence/check/ .

### Objections

**P1** (high). Where: Step 2, blocks A and B; section 6, decision 6; section 8, bullet 2 ('The end is looked at again if the step rule changes'); step 5 ('The driver is frozen here')

- Problem: Decision 6 says blocks A and B decide the driver's step rule. The only written pass rule is in block A: model E on sheet 03, a sheet it was trained on. Block B has 'no pass rule'. No block is said to drive a new step rule through a loop. No block tests what the driver does after the first stop picture. Section 2.2 of the plan names both dangers itself (run 7's loop used the stretched steps; the stop comes about 0.8 cm earlier). After session 2 the driver is frozen, so a rule chosen on sheet 03 can break sheet 05, which works today, and leave the line's end uncovered. It also breaks the plan's own point 5 of section 1 (a change is kept only if it helps on tracks the model has not trained on, by a rule written before the result).
- Evidence: CHECK, step length, challenge 3 [measured offline by the checker]: on 11.11 the all-sheets models shortened their steps at bends (0.46 to 0.51 cm), models that never saw the sheet hardly do (0.63 cm at bends, 0.70 cm on straight parts), 'so a win of B on sheet 03 would flatter the rule for the exam's unseen line'. Same section, challenge 6: after the short runs 'full runs for the best one or two settings on sheet 05 and the unseen track. The decision rule must include the stop place and the unseen track, because sheet 03 is a training sheet'. The plan took the first half of this design and dropped sheet 05 and the rule. CHECK, dashed line, challenge 2: 'Test it on sheet 05 first, on the same day as a fixed-step baseline'. I read the log again (e12_run_endings_and_small_numbers.out.txt) [measured]: in run 7, steps 47 to 55, the model asked 2.28 cm in all and the driver moved 8.98 cm while the forearm turned from -1.9 to +50.5 degrees. CHECK, step length, verdict 20 (corrected) and stop and centring, challenge 3 [calculated offline]: with the model's own length the confirming move is about 0.2 cm, the puck stops about 5 mm short at the median and 35 of 103 stay more than 7 mm short; step length, challenge 2: 'Do not take it to the robot without deciding what the driver does after the first stop picture'. CHECK, bends, verdict 11 (corrected) [calculated, first order]: with model E's asked lengths the tip advances 3.8 to 5.3 cm at the bend against 6.0 to 8.9 cm in the eight standard-start recordings, so E at its own length may cut the bend on the inside; for models that never saw the sheet the twist checker finds factor 1.0 right (twist, challenge 1). So block A with model E alone can mislead both ways. CHECK, twist, missed 8 and step length, challenge 3 [checkers' judgement]: the shorter fixed step is a control, 'not a candidate for the final driver'; the plan's risk table makes it the fall-back driver. CHECK, step length, verdict 16 (corrected) [measured and calculated]: the own length needs 1.2 to 1.3 times the pictures on easy sheets and about twice on sheet 03; a fixed 0.65 cm needs 1.5 times. Time is the second exam criterion.
- The critic's fix: Replace blocks A and B by: 'A. First bend of sheet 03, short runs ended with Esc: fixed 1.0 cm, the model's own length, fixed 0.65 cm, each with model E and with the model trained without sheet 03 (4 runs per cell, 24 short runs, about 40 minutes). If fixed 1.0 cm passes 2 or more of 4 from the controlled start, the start was the cause and the step rule stays. B. Every setting that survives A, with fixed 1.0 cm as the control on the same day: 3 full runs on sheet 03 with the model trained without it, 3 full runs on sheet 05 with model E, 3 on one new track. Rule written before the session: a new step rule replaces fixed 1.0 cm only if (1) it loses the line in fewer of the unseen-line runs, (2) it finishes sheet 05's loop at least as often as fixed 1.0 cm, and (3) its stops, read from the photos, are not shorter than those of fixed 1.0 cm by more than 3 mm. End-game arm: with the own length, 3 runs with the confirming move as asked and 3 with the confirming move kept at 1.0 cm (needs the professor's yes to question 5). The shorter fixed step is a control; it becomes a driver only if it passes (2) and its time cost (1.5 times the pictures) is accepted.'
- Check: **partly**. The objection itself stands in full; 'partly' is for the fix. Checked: the plan (step 2 blocks A to C, decision 6, section 8 bullet 2, section 9) and CHECK. All the evidence named is there and quoted correctly [looked up]: step length challenge 6 ('full runs for the best one or two settings on sheet 05 and the unseen track. The decision rule must include the stop place and the unseen track, because sheet 03 is a training sheet'), challenges 2 and 3, verdicts 16 and 20; dashed line challenge 2 (run 7's walk used the stretched steps; I reran e12: asked 2.28 cm, driven 8.98 cm over steps 47 to 55 [measured]); stop and centring challenge 3 (with the model's own length about 5 mm short at the median, 35 of 103 more than 7 mm short [calculated offline by the checker]); bends verdict 11 (3.8 to 5.3 cm against 6.0 to 8.9 cm [calculated]). In the plan the only written pass rule for the driver is block A (model E on sheet 03), block B has 'no pass rule', block C uses 'the driver of 11.11', and the end is put off. Two small slips of the critic: the plan freezes the driver at session 3, not after session 2 (step 5 and the calendar), so sheet 05 would be seen once more, but together with a new model; and block B already holds an unseen line (model E on two new tracks), only without a rule or a named driver setting. What is wrong in the fix: (1) 'if fixed 1.0 cm passes 2 or more of 4, the start was the cause and the step rule stays' cannot be drawn from four runs. On 11.11 the bend was passed 1 time in 3; with that rate 2 or more of 4 happens by chance in 41 % of cases [calculated]. It would also close the question that block B is there to answer. (2) 'stops not shorter by more than 3 mm' cannot be read from 3 stops per setting when stops scatter by 4 to 6 mm (stop and centring challenge 3) and a photo is good to about 3 mm (the critic's own P2). (3) 'loses the line in fewer runs' has no margin. (4) The fix about doubles the session and gives no order.
- The checker's fix: Block A (first bend of sheet 03, short runs ended with Esc, tape marks, controlled start, taking turns): model E with fixed 1.0 cm, the model's own length and fixed 0.65 cm, 4 runs each; plus two controls at fixed 1.0 cm: the team of five and the model trained without sheet 03, 4 runs each. 20 short runs, about 45 minutes [guess]. Block A only sorts the settings: one goes on with at least 3 of 4 passes against at most 1 of 4 for fixed 1.0 cm (a setting that is no better meets this in about 7 % of cases [calculated, pass rate 1 in 3]). If fixed 1.0 cm itself passes 2 or more of 4, the bend cannot tell the settings apart: write that down and let block B decide alone; do not conclude that the start was the cause. Block B decides the driver, with fixed 1.0 cm always run as the control on the same day: for each setting 3 full runs on sheet 03 with the model trained without it, 3 on sheet 05 with model E, 3 on one new track with a loop. Rule written before the session: a new step rule replaces fixed 1.0 cm only if (1) it loses the line in at least 2 fewer of the 6 unseen-line runs, (2) it finishes sheet 05's loop at least as often, and (3) of the runs that reach the end it covers the end at least as often (ruler photo; centres at most 7 mm apart, 11 mm when the puck stands straight beyond the end). Otherwise fixed 1.0 cm stays and the question is run again with more runs in session 3. End of the line: the move after the first stop picture stays 1.0 cm whatever the step rule (today's behaviour); the as-asked move (about 0.2 cm, about 8 mm earlier [calculated by the checker]) is tried in 3 runs on sheet 01 only if time is left; both need the professor's answer. The shorter fixed step is a control; it becomes the driver only if it meets (1) to (3) and its time (about 1.5 times the pictures) is accepted. Pictures and seconds per run are written beside every result. Blocks C and D move to a later session.

**P2** (high). Where: Section 3, paragraph under the table ('agreed with a second, independent measure to 1 to 2 mm') and the 'lost' rule in the table; step 1 point 1; pass rules of blocks A and D; block E; step 3 rule 2

- Problem: The plan makes a picture measure the judge of blocks A to D and of every new recording. Its '1 to 2 mm' holds on sheets 01 and 02 and at the four stops only. On the sheets with sharp bends and loops, where it has to judge, the measure's own two variants disagree about the plan's limits in a fifth to a half of the pictures. Applied to the logs of 11.11 the plan's own rules give wrong verdicts: block A's rule fails run 10, the one run that passed the bend, and the 'lost' rule marks three finished runs as lost. Block E checks the measure only at stops, where it is at its best, and its 3 mm allowance is smaller than the error of a slightly slanted photo of a 2.9 cm tall puck.
- Evidence: CHECK, stop and centring, verdict 12 (corrected): on the recordings the one-picture and the 12-picture measure differ by a median 3.1 mm and by more than 5 mm in 449 of 1,298 pictures; the sign flips; 'safely real' does not hold on sheet 06. Verdict 9: the checker's own tool 'fails inside the loop'. CHECK, dashed line, verdict 1 and missed 9: the line placed from pictures with the forearm twisted over 60 degrees lies 5.6 to 7.4 mm (10.2 at most) from the line placed from little-twisted pictures. Stop and centring, challenge 1: the ruler check 'should come before it is built in or used to accept and reject anything'. Mine, from the analyst's tables [measured]: e11_measure_agreement_strict.out.txt reproduces 1,298 / 3.1 mm / 449 and splits it by sheet: median difference 1.7 mm on sheets 01 and 02, then 4.2 (03), 7.7 (04), 2.9 (05), 4.3 mm (06); the two variants disagree on 'over 12 mm' in 10, 4, 21, 46, 15, 20 % of pictures and on 'within 7 mm' in 6, 12, 29, 52, 28, 30 %. e05_measure_agreement_and_lost_rule.out.txt and e05b_jumps_by_size.out.txt [measured]: run 10 reads 19.0 and 14.7 mm off at pictures 4 and 5 (both flagged clean and aligned), then 1.2 mm at picture 6; in the pictures the puck sits on the ink (e06_run_10_pictures_002_009.jpg, looked at by me), so block A's rule fails run 10 at picture 5. The size of the offset jumps by more than 12 mm between two pictures 4 times in run 5, 8 in run 6, 7 in run 7, twice in run 10; the puck moves at most 10 mm per step. Section 3's 'lost' rule, read as more than 17 mm from the line's middle twice in a row, marks run 1 as lost at picture 23, run 4 at 25 and run 7 at 49, and dates run 5's loss at picture 9 where the view-novelty checker measured 18 (read as 12 mm from the middle it is harsher: run 4 at 21, run 7 at 27, run 10 at 5). e04_block_a_rule_details.out.txt [measured]: of the three sampled standard-start recordings of sheet 03, one fails block A's rule by the 12-picture variant (plan 03-02: 13.9, 13.1, 12.4 mm at pictures 19 to 21) and all three by the one-picture variant (plan 03-25: 14.5 to 17.6 mm at pictures 5 to 9; plan 03-13: 16.5 to 22.3 mm at pictures 12 to 16). Block E [calculated]: a phone 6 degrees off the vertical shifts the top of the 29 mm tall puck by 29 x tan 6 = 3 mm. Blocks A and D give no stops and unseen runs may not finish, so 10 stops are not sure.
- The critic's fix: Replace the paragraph by: 'Two picture measures agree to a median 1 to 2 mm on sheets 01 and 02 and at the four stops. On sheets 03 to 06 the measure's two variants differ by a median 3 to 8 mm and disagree about the 12 mm limit in 15 to 46 % of pictures; it fails inside loops, on dashes and where two pieces of line lie close together [measured]. Until checked it is a help, not a judge.' Add to step 1: 'Before session 2 the scoring command is run on the ten logged runs and must give: run 10 passes the first bend, runs 5 and 9 fail it. The offset is smoothed (median of three pictures, no jump above 10 mm per step, sizes only, no sign). The pass rule of block A is fixed on these logs. A run that leaves the line and comes back (runs 4 and 7) is scored as left for N pictures, not as lost.' Add: 'At bends and loops two people give the verdict from the sheet of small pictures; the number stands beside it.' Make block E its own block: 'model E, driver of 11.11, sheet 01 five times and sheet 02 as recorded five times: 10 stops for the ruler check and the first repeat numbers. A pencil line is drawn round the puck's foot before it is lifted, or the photo is kept only if no side wall of the puck shows.' Add to block 0: 'the puck is laid by hand 0, 7, 12 and 20 mm beside the line (marks drawn with a ruler) on a straight part and at the first bend of sheet 03 with the forearm turned; the measure must read these within 3 mm.'
- Check: **stands**. Checked the analyst's own tables in stop_and_centring (robot_runs_steps.csv, demonstration_centring_0X.csv) through the critic's e04, e05, e05b and e11 (e05 and e11 rerun: identical), and CHECK, stop and centring verdicts 8, 9, 12 and challenge 1, dashed line verdict 1 and missed 9. Everything named is there [measured]: pooled 1,298 pictures, median 3.1 mm, 449 over 5 mm; by sheet a median of 1.7 mm on sheets 01 and 02 and 4.2, 7.7, 2.9, 4.3 mm on sheets 03 to 06; the two variants disagree about 'over 12 mm' in 21, 46, 15 and 20 % of pictures there. Run 10 reads 19.0 and 14.7 mm at pictures 4 and 5, both flagged clean and aligned; on the critic's sheet e06_run_10_pictures_002_009.jpg the puck sits on the ink in both [my own eyes]. The 'lost' rule (17 mm from the middle twice in a row) marks run 1 at picture 23, run 4 at 25 and run 7 at 49 [measured]. Three things to add. (1) The '1 to 2 mm' reaches a little further than 'sheets 01 and 02 and four stops': the dashed-line pair of pipelines agrees to a median 1.4 mm on run 8 (CHECK, dashed line verdict 5). Even with those two readings thrown out, run 10 reads 12.5 to 15.6 mm at pictures 21 to 22 while still on the ink, so '12 mm from the middle' is tight as a pass rule. - The checker's fix: Replace the paragraph under the table of section 3 by: 'Two independent picture measures agree to a median of 1 to 2 mm on sheets 01 and 02, at the four stops and on run 8's puck path, but they share the fitted camera. On sheets 03 to 06 the measure's own two variants differ by a median of 3 to 8 mm and disagree about the 12 mm limit in 15 to 46 % of pictures; lines placed from far-twisted and little-twisted views lie 6 to 7 mm apart; it fails inside loops and on dashes [measured]. Until it has passed the checks below it is a help, not a judge.' Step 1, acceptance test before session 2: run on the ten logged runs, the scoring command must agree with what the checkers saw: run 10 on the ink through the first bend, runs 5 and 9 off it from picture 17 or 18, run 6 off from picture 10, run 2 on the line all the way. The pass rule of block A is then fixed on these logs and on the eight standard-start recordings of sheet 03, which must pass it (or the rule is stricter than the teacher). How wrong single readings are removed is left to the tool; a median of three is not enough. It reports sizes only, pushed pictures apart from standing ones, and three states: on the line, left and came back (how many pictures), lost. Block A's rule in words a person can check: the puck still touches the ink in every picture to 10 cm past the bend; two people decide from the sheet of small pictures, the number stands beside it. The same at loops and on dashes. Block 0, added: with the arm standing still (forearm straight on a straight part; forearm turned at the first bend of sheet 03) the puck is laid by hand 0, 7, 12 and 20 mm beside the line on a spare print with ruler marks; the measure must read these within 3 mm; where it fails it does not judge (and the camera is refitted inside the measuring script, not in config.py). Block E as a block of its own: model E, driver of 11.11, sheet 01 five times and sheet 02 as recorded five times: 10 stops and the first repeat numbers. The photo is taken with the puck in the middle of the photo and no side wall of the puck showing (6 degrees off shifts the puck's top by 3 mm [calculated]). No pencil marks on a sheet that is used again.

**P3** (high). Where: Step 2 (no block holds sheet 04); section 2.1, row of run 6; section 1 point 2 ('the model itself turning too little at sharp bends' as second cause); step 3 table, row 'The six course sheets in the lay-outs session 2 found weak'

CHECK, view novelty, verdict 8 (corrected): the least familiar start of the day (percentile 92, 92, 89), 5.0 cm from the recorded start; the same verdict also supports the model reading: on the five nearest training pictures of step 8 model E answers 0.58 cm at 0 degrees where the labels' mean is +47. Both readings stand; the plan states one. **P4** (high). Where: Step 3, rules 1, 3, 4 and 6; step 4, third bullet; section 6, decisions 5 and 7

- Problem: The demonstration rules are the heart of the most expensive step. As written they (a) fix the start before block D has tested it, although decision 5 says it is decided after block D, (b) describe a way round a sharp bend that the 209 old recordings do not use and that the arm cannot do where the line runs back towards the base, (c) need a change of the recording tool that the plan does not name, and (d) do not say what happens to the old recordings. Three checkers warn that a second style beside the first re-creates 'same place, different ways', which is the plan's own cause 4.
- Evidence: Two styles: CHECK, view novelty, challenge 5 ('leaves two routines for the same places unless the old rows are dropped, and it tunes the data to the six course sheets, which Emranul ruled out'); dashed line, challenge 8 ('one style only'); bends, challenge 8, on a change of the recording tool ('a change would put two label styles into one training set'). The old style at sharp bends is already consistent [measured]: bends, verdict 3: all 15 passes of sheet 03's first bend from end A twist the same way, hairpin 1 the same way in 96 % of 26 passes. And it is not 'turn in place until the line runs up the picture': twist, verdict 7: the travel direction is a median 46.4 degrees off the picture's top and beyond 90 degrees in 24 % of steps; twist, verdict 5: the tip really moves round the puck while the forearm turns (the recorded move lies a median 124 degrees from the swing a pure twist gives). A pure twist does not bring the tip behind the puck for the new direction [my reading of these two verdicts]. Limits of the arm [calculated and measured]: orientation, verdicts 7 and 8: with joint 4 at 117 degrees the picture's top turns at most 106 / 112 / 116 / 123 degrees from 'straight away' at 12 / 25 / 35 / 45 cm; a line running straight back to the base lies 57 to 72 degrees off the picture's top at best; the demonstrators looked along such lines in 0 to 1 % of rows. So rule 3 cannot be followed in hairpins and loops. Rule 1: orientation, challenge 2 and missed 4: for run 3's lay-out the pre-turned start needs joint 4 at -82 to -97 degrees at 17.5 to 18.3 cm reach; no recording starts with the forearm turned 40 degrees or more within 25 cm of the base; only 153 pictures (0.8 %) lie within 20 cm with the forearm turned 60 degrees or more; 'a hand-set twist of about 90 degrees per sheet with the motors off is slow and easy to get wrong at the exam'. Rule 6: wrist, verdict 4: Emranul's wrist moves over a median range of 8.0 degrees per episode, so it cannot be held at 13.5 by hand; wrist, challenge 4: solving the replay with the wrist at 13.5 'changes the Recording workstream's file and needs its own robot check', and the rod then leans 6 to 10 degrees more at far reach (42 degrees at 40 cm against 36 with wrist 5) [calculated]. Dashed line, verdict 9 [measured]: at the walk round the puck 12 of 13 recordings have the wrist above 18.5 degrees (median 25.3), so the hand trial must first show that the walk can be done with the wrist held.

Until then the sheet is laid so that the line leaves straight away from the base, the tip about 5 cm behind the puck, the forearm straight (148 of the 209 recordings start so; such starts score 0.99 offline on an unseen sheet). 3. Sharp bends: keep the recorded way (the tip goes round the outside while the forearm turns). A new way is recorded only where the old recordings disagree with each other: the square start, sheet 04's first S-bend, the loops. 4. Loops: the hand trial must show the chosen way three times in a row with the wrist at 13.5 degrees and the forearm inside 117 degrees; if it cannot, the rule is dropped. 6. The wrist is held by the tool, not by the hand: the replay is solved with wrist 13.5 and hand -6.1. This change belongs to the Recording and data session, goes through the orchestrator and gets its own robot check (rod lean at far reach).' **P10** (medium). Where: Section 1 point 4 ('the expensive one that the evidence says matters most for an unseen line'); step 3, first paragraph; step 4 (the 5-point rule)

- Problem: 'The evidence says matters most' is stronger than the evidence. The number behind it comes from a small test on frozen features with one to five sheets. Mine [calculated by carrying that fit beyond its range, so a guess]: 6 to 18 shapes is 1.58 doublings, +4.0 points (2.5 to 5.4); 48 episodes add about 4,100 pictures to 18,032, 0.3 doublings, +0.5 points. The new shapes get 4 episodes each; the report's smallest case was a quarter of a sheet's episodes. What the robot test adds is qualitative: CHECK, dashed line, missed 8 and twist, verdict 12 (manoeuvres tied to the sheet they were learnt on).
- The critic's fix: Section 1 point 4 and step 3: 'more different tracks is the best-supported lever for an unseen line, but its size is a guess: about 4 points by the report's fit, carried beyond its range [calculated].' Make the recording staged: 'Batch 1: 4 new shapes, 16 episodes. Retrain; score on the fixed pictures of the ten runs and on robot runs on shapes not recorded; go on with 8 more shapes only if the gain is at least what the report measured per doubling. Stop examples are recorded on purpose, because more shapes were not shown to help the stop.'
- Check: **partly**. Checked the earlier report, section 8.2 (lines 1462 to 1490) and 8.4 [looked up]: +2.5 points per doubling of different sheets at equal pictures (+1.6 to +3.4), +1.7 per doubling of pictures, 'frozen features with a names head ... one to five training sheets, so anything beyond six is an extrapolation', 'For the stop ... more sheets were not shown to help', and in 8.4 'new tracks are the better bet, not a sure one'. The plan's 'the step the evidence says matters most' is firmer than its own source. The critic's sums are right [calculated]: 6 to 18 shapes is 1.58 doublings, about +4 points; 48 episodes add about 0.3 doublings of pictures, +0.5. That part stands. Two parts do not hold as written. 'Less than the 5 points the plan itself asks of a change': the 5-point rule is for training settings, not for new data, so the two are not one yardstick. And the staged gate ('go on with 8 more shapes only if the gain is at least what the report measured per doubling') cannot be read: 4 shapes promise about +2 points, and the report itself calls such gains 'below the 3.3-point spread of single runs'; the pictures of the ten runs have no labels to score against; three robot runs cannot show 2 points. How the round is judged is written before recording: the six course sheets scored as unseen sheets by models trained with and without the new shapes (same recipe, 3 seeds each, the seed spread shown), the stop per sheet, the summed turn at sharp bends, and robot runs on the same new tracks and driver as in session 2. ### Missing from the plan, by the critic

1. How the tape marks 'where the sheet lay in the recordings' are found (step 1 point 3). There is no single place per sheet: sheet 03 end A has two placement groups 11 to 11.5 degrees apart, sheet 04 two groups 7.5 to 8.5 degrees apart (CHECK, step length, verdict 17), sheet 05 from end B six placements over 51 degrees (dashed line, verdict 19); the set-up pictures show the planned place, not the real one (start caps at 19.2 cm, drawn at 16.4; sheets a median 5.9 degrees from the plan: orientation, verdict 4 and analyst's correction 5); nine of ten starts on 11.11 lay 1.3 to 3.7 cm to the left (view novelty, missed 5); wrist, challenge 5: with a taught pose and a displaced sheet 'the tip starts beside the puck, worse than a hand-placed tip'. A cheap method to write into the plan [my proposal]: the arm stands at the recorded start pose, the live picture is laid over the first picture of the chosen recording, the sheet is slid until the lines meet, then taped.
2. A reach rule for the print set and for every new lay-out. The offline direction score falls to 0.56 to 0.63 beyond 42.5 cm (orientation, verdict 12), the driver ends a run when the rod leans over 58 degrees at about 47.5 cm (wrist, verdict 11; model_mode.py lines 293 to 294), and one practice track already comes within 0.1 cm of that limit on the centre cross (orientation, verdict 14). The print set of step 1 point 4 and the 12 new shapes need a stated largest reach and a check of the table edge (orientation, challenge 3).
3. A refit of the camera model before the scoring command is built. The fitted camera dates from 6.11.26 on the old desk (orientation, analyst's open question 7); the camera stepped on 8.11.26; after the step the analyst's correction repairs only part (a standing puck wanders 2.6 mm against 1.5 mm before: stop and centring, verdict 11). Challenge 7 of that section: keep the correction inside the measuring script, do not patch config.py, 'do the proper refit on pictures from 9.11.26 on, with Emranul's go'.
4. A two-minute look, motors off and the arm held, at which part of the block touches the puck (wrist, verdict 13 'unverifiable' and challenge 8). Stop and centring, missed 7: the puck's distance from the tip falls from 20 to 13 mm along run 2 and is about 40 mm at the end of run 4, 'where the leaning rod pushes the puck's top edge'. This bears directly on Emranul's wish to keep the puck in the middle.
5. Which session changes which file. HOW_WE_WORK.md gives the recording tool to the Recording and data session and robot trials to Evaluation; stop and centring, challenge 1: the recording page part 'goes through the orchestrator'. The plan's rule 2 (offset shown after every episode), rule 6 (wrist held by the replay) and the correction key of step 5 are all changes of the recording tool.
6. A third set of tracks for robot tests between session 2 and the final test. config.py TEST_TRACK_PARAMETERS already knows three sets (practice 6, validation 4, test 10). The plan names development tracks and a sealed set and does not say whether the 12 training shapes of step 3 include the six development tracks. If they do, session 3 and the correction rounds have no unseen track left except the sealed one, against point 5 of section 1.
7. Questions for the professor that are open in 05_meetings/requests_to_professor.md and not in section 7: question 18 (how the start is set at the exam; may the robot find it itself), question 7 (what is handed in on 10.1.27; it sizes the documentation weeks) and question 10 (must the policy run offline or locally). Stop and centring, challenge 6: bring a top-view photo of a stop, and 'ask how a stop that never comes, or comes early, is judged'.
8. Label fixes before any retraining: frames.csv start_end is stale for 30 episodes (wrist, analyst's correction 4; dashed line, verdict 20: use corrected_ends.csv); the sheet-01 offset labels of plans 01-17 and 01-18 are swapped (view novelty, verdict 18); tracks.json lists 7 of 20 tracks; the two input files that every 'kind of place' rests on have no script and no original in the project (bends, missed 6).
9. A per-picture check of the dash-cutting augmentation named in step 4: dashed line, verdict 23: 'Feasible, but every edited picture needs a check before it is trained on' (the band must be shifted per picture by up to 14 px and the robot's own parts need a proper mask).
10. The time cost of the step rules as a line in the decision: the model's own length needs 1.2 to 1.3 times the pictures on easy sheets and about twice on sheet 03, at 1.08 s per picture (step length, verdict 16); a fixed 0.65 cm needs 1.5 times [calculated]. Time is the second exam criterion and is ranked against the other groups (professor, 26.10.26).
11. Whether the puck follows pushes of 2 to 5 mm. Block 0 is a dry run without the puck; twist, missed 6: 'nobody has seen whether the puck moves cleanly under such small pushes', and the arm's own error of about 0.05 cm is up to a quarter of a 0.2 cm step.
12. The picture familiarity number as a log with the controlled start. Two checkers found the robot's pictures less like the recordings all day, good runs and failed alike (view novelty, verdict 16: median percentile 65, 1.6 to 1.8 times the local spacing; orientation, missed 3: 0.67 to 0.84 against 0.93). If the gap stays with the controlled start, the day (light, table, camera step) is the reason, not the start.

### The same list, checked

1. Tape marks (how the recorded place of a sheet is found): really missing and it matters, because every comparison of session 2 rests on it; the placement figures are quoted correctly. But the critic's picture overlay misplaces sheets 01 to 03 and most of 04: the camera stepped 15 to 17 px on 8.11.26, so the two pictures must first be laid so that the robot's own fork coincides, or the sheet is placed by the arm's tip at two recorded points and the overlay is only the check [my proposal].
2. Reach rule for the prints and lay-outs: really missing and it matters (a run ends near 47.5 cm with a straight forearm; one practice track comes within 0.1 cm of that; the table edge has not been checked).
3. Refit of the camera before the scoring command: really missing; it matters only if the measure is to judge at bends. Do the hand-laid puck check first and refit inside the measuring script, not in config.py, if it fails there.
4. Two-minute look at which part of the block touches the puck: really missing, cheap, and it bears on centring (by the analyst's places the puck sits 13 to 40 mm from the tip); motors off, one person holds the arm the whole time.
5. Which session changes which file: really missing; HOW_WE_WORK.md gives the recording tool to the Recording and data session, so rules 2 and 6 and the correction key go through the orchestrator.
6. A third set of tracks: really unclear in the plan and it matters for point 5 of section 1; config.py already names three sets (practice 6, validation 4, test 10). The plan must say that the new tracks used for robot tests are never recorded on, and that corrections are recorded on training shapes only.
7. Open questions for the professor: question 18 is really missing and matters (the controlled start is a scripted move). Question 7 only sizes the documentation weeks; question 10 changes nothing in this plan; 'how is a stop that never comes or comes early judged' is half covered by the plan's question 3.
8. Label fixes: real, but none of them touches training (the training code already uses the corrected ends, offset labels are not inputs, tracks.json is already in 2.4). It is housekeeping for the next rebuild of the index, not something to do 'before any retraining'.
9. Check of the dash-cutting pictures: really missing and it matters once they are trained on. Every picture cannot be looked at, so: one number per picture (the cut band within a few px of the line) plus a looked-at sample.
10. Time cost of the step rules as a line in the decision: really missing; it matters a little (the professor ranks finishing far above time); the figures are quoted correctly.
11. Do pushes of 2 to 5 mm move the puck: half missing. Block A's own-length runs will show it anyway; add one own-length run on sheet 01 with the puck straight after the dry run, which also gives the first stop place with short steps.
12. Familiarity number as a log: really missing, cheap if computed afterwards from the saved pictures (not inside the driver); a help for the 'start or day' question, not a judge.

### Missed by the critic

1. Section 1 point 1, and the critic's own corrected sentence in P5: 'four reached the line's end' is kinder than the plan's own first aim. By the 12 mm rule two of the ten runs ended on the line's end (runs 4 and 7); run 2 missed it by about 1 mm (13 mm) and run 1 stood 21 mm beside it, clear of the ink (CHECK, stop and centring verdict 14 and the analyst's correction 5) [measured by the checker, no ruler check]. Corrected sentence: 'Four runs brought the puck to the end of the line and stopped by themselves; by the 12 mm rule two of them stopped on the end (runs 4 and 7). Five of the six lost runs also stopped by themselves, at a wrong place.' This is the baseline every later session is compared with.
2. Section 2.2, cause 4, counts 'a walk round the puck inside the loops' as 'same place, different ways'. The check says the opposite: all 13 recordings of sheet 06 from end A and all 18 of sheet 05 from end B make the walk (CHECK, dashed line verdicts 7 and 8) [measured]. It is one way; what differs is the style between sheets 03 and 04 and the loop sheets (bends verdict 5). So rule 4 of step 3 ('or unwinding the forearm early') would bring a second way into the loops where there is one. Corrected: 'four ways round the puck at the square start, two ways through sheet 04's first S-bend; in the loops all recordings walk round the puck, but often with the puck beside the line'.
3. Step 3 does not tie what is recorded to decision 6. CHECK, stop and centring challenge 4: recoveries in which the tip goes round a standing puck 'cannot be driven by the present fixed 1 cm step; more of them help only once the driver can make small steps'. The same holds for the turn at sharp bends and the walk: 27 % of the recorded steps are under 0.7 cm and carry 44 % of all twist (twist verdict 5) [measured]. Add to step 3: 'If fixed 1.0 cm stays the driver, recoveries and bends are recorded only in a way a 1 cm step can carry out; the 30 recovery episodes wait for decision 6.'
4. Section 7, question 5, has lost the question that can decide decision 6. The earlier report (2026-11-09_training_set_up_analysis.md, 10.1, question 1) asked: 'Do a fixed 1-cm step, clamps and a step budget count as rules outside the model?' The plan now asks only whether the program 'may multiply the model's step by a fixed number'. Two of block A's three settings, the fall-back driver and the move after the first stop picture are fixed steps that ignore the model's length, and the controlled start is a scripted move (question 18) [looked up]. Add to question 5: 'May the program move a fixed distance in the model's direction and ignore the model's length? May it always add one fixed move after the first stop picture? May a scripted move bring the arm to the start pose?' It costs nothing and goes out now with step 0.
5. Step 4 judges every candidate by '5 points of path within 45 degrees'. The check found this number blind to what failed on 11.11: offline, bends are not where the direction is wrong (0.79 to 0.80 at sharp bends and hairpins, bends verdict 7), and model E and the team of five are level there (0.780 against 0.780 to 0.794, bends challenge 7) although they answer 15 to 17 degrees apart on the robot's pictures of sheet 03's bend (c02) [calculated]. The numbers that did describe the failures are not in the rule: the share of the recorded turn given back over 10 steps (0.52, twist verdict 10), the net turn per cm in sharp stretches (0.63 to 0.68, step length verdict 12) and the stop per sheet. Add them to the rule of step 4, each with its limit written before the result; otherwise a candidate that cures the turning can fail the rule and one that does not can pass.

### To keep

## Can robot session 2 and its tools answer their questions? (folder `robot_session`)

**The critic's verdict.** Not sound yet under this lens, but repairable without more robot hours. The plan's instincts are right (a control beside every question, rules written first, a model that never saw the sheet, a start like the recordings), yet as written blocks 0, A, B, C, D and E cannot answer their questions. Three changes matter most. (1) Block A's rule cannot be scored as worded: applied to the three sheet-03 runs of 11.11 with the measure that exists it gives 0 passes of 3 where the check found 1 of 3 [measured]; and with 4 runs per setting a setting that is no better is taken on with a chance of up to 12 to 17 %, while a really better one (1 in 3 up to 2 in 3) is taken on only 35 % of the time [calculated]. (2) The exam case is tried only on sheet 03, where the model that never saw the sheet answers almost like model E, with no rule, no old-driver run and 3 runs per setting; sheet 05, where seen and unseen differ most, is left out. (3) The measure is checked last (block E) although the rules of A, C and D use it; the stall rule of step 1 ends 8 to 14 % of healthy runs once steps are short [calculated]; and the controlled start is neither one place (recorded lay-outs of one sheet and end spread over 23 degrees and 2.5 to 3 cm [measured]) nor code that exists. Time: the plan's runs alone take 2.4 to 4.2 hours at the pace of 11.11 [calculated from logs], four to five hours with set-up [guess]. Leanest order that still answers the questions: day 1 (about 2 hours, no new track prints needed): step check, measure check at known places, sheet 01 as known-good control, block A with the unseen-sheet model moved into it. Day 2 (about 2.5 hours): the stop on sheet 01 under the chosen step, sheet 05 seen against unseen, two new tracks, a sheet-04 repeat, then D. Cut C first (no decision of section 6 waits for it), then D. Feasibility read in the code [measured]: the fold models load and run through the whole loop (all six checkpoints and metrics.json exist); --step-length predicted, --length-gain, --twist-gain, --step-budget exist; the fixed step's length, the stall numbers, any drive to a start pose, a PDF writer, a given centre line and tight bends in make_test_tracks.py are new work. The earlier attempt's six scripts were read, found sound and reused; its stall count was recomputed with the same result. Paths used below: REVIEW = D:/AI_Project/.claude/worktrees/training-analysis/06_deliverables/research_notes/Model and training/evidence/robot_test_2026-11-11/plan_review/robot_session/ (my scripts and their .out.txt files), EVIDENCE = D:/AI_Project/.claude/worktrees/training-analysis/06_deliverables/research_notes/Model and training/evidence/robot_test_2026-11-11/, CODE = D:/AI_Project/.claude/worktrees/training-analysis/02_code/, NOTES = D:/AI_Project/.claude/worktrees/training-analysis/06_deliverables/research_notes/.

**The checker's overall verdict.** Most of the critique stands. I tested all 15 objections against the files: 9 stand (P1, P7, P9, P10, P11, P12, P13, P14, P15), 6 are right in direction but wrong in size or in their fix (P2, P3, P4, P5, P6, P8), none falls. Every quote from the verified results that I looked up is in the file as given, the code facts are right (driver flags, hand-placed start, guards, track generator, fold models), and three of the critic's scripts give the same output when run again (loading the fold models, the stall count, the runs on the simulated robot). Four fixes must be replaced. (1) P2: the rank rule on the largest distance is weaker than the plan's own rule when runs fall into two groups (kept, about 6 mm; lost, about 35 mm), which is what the three runs and five demonstrations in hand look like: it takes on a real step from 0.2 to 0.8 with a chance of 0.48 against 0.67 [simulated]. A count rule with 5 runs each ('at least 3 more runs keep the line') takes a no-better setting on with at most 0.055, that real step with 0.68, and covers every outcome [calculated]. (2) P5: the critic's stall rule of 25 pictures still ends 42 to 52 of 209 healthy walks at the model's own length when the demonstrations are walked at the model's pace [calculated]; a rule that counts path instead of pictures ends none at fixed steps, 4 to 8 at the own length (real hesitations) and still ends run 6. (3) P3: 'one step for all of block B' drops the fixed-step baseline on sheet 05 that the verified results ask for by name, because the one loop that worked relied on the stretched steps. (4) P4 and P6: one snapshot cannot give the scorer's run measure (it needs two views of the bare line), and the stored camera reference and every demonstration of sheets 01 to 03 are from before the camera moved on 8.11.26, so the camera check and the ghost need a new reference [measured]. One fix came out stronger than the critic showed: sheet 03's traced centre line, laid onto each run's own pictures, gives fail, fail, pass for runs 5, 9 and 10 at every window from 6 to 10 cm and at 12 and 17 mm, also from the first 18 to 27 pictures alone [measured by me; no ruler check, and the camera model is shared]. Nothing was connected to the robot, no existing file was changed, no git change was made, nothing was trained. Paths used below: CHECK = D:/AI_Project/.claude/worktrees/training-analysis/06_deliverables/research_notes/Model and training/evidence/robot_test_2026-11-11/plan_review/robot_session/check/ (my scripts and their .out.txt files), REVIEW = its parent folder (the critic's scripts), EVIDENCE = D:/AI_Project/.claude/worktrees/training-analysis/06_deliverables/research_notes/Model and training/evidence/robot_test_2026-11-11/, CODE = D:/AI_Project/.claude/worktrees/training-analysis/02_code/.

### Objections

**P1** (high). Where: Step 2, block A, column 'Rule fixed in advance'; section 3, row 'stays in the middle'; step 1 item 1 (the scoring command); the same wording in block D

- Problem: The pass rule ('within 12 mm of the line's middle to 10 cm past the bend') cannot be scored as worded with the tool that exists, and it is not the plan's own meaning of 'lost' (section 3: more than 12 mm outside the ink, which is 17 mm from the middle). The tool knows no 'bend' and no '10 cm past'. Near a bend it marks many of its own readings as doubtful, and those are the very pictures in which the puck is lost; one badly wrong reading it does not mark at all. So the rule gives wrong answers on runs we already know. The 10 cm window also ends where the next bend of sheet 03 begins.
- Evidence: [measured] REVIEW/block_a_rule_on_the_three_runs.out.txt and block_a_rule_windows.out.txt, computed from EVIDENCE/stop_and_centring/robot_runs_steps.csv (offset_mm of run_measures.measure_steps). Run 10, which the check says passed the first bend, fails the plan's rule: 19.0 and 14.7 mm at pictures 4 and 5 (the line is followed back 17 mm under the puck there) and 12.5 and 15.6 mm at pictures 21 and 22, 9 to 10 cm past the bend. So the old driver scores 0 of 3, not 1 of 3. Doubtful readings in the window: 13 of 26 pictures (run 5), 10 of 28 (run 9), 5 of 23 (run 10). If they are left out, run 9 passes up to 6 cm past the bend although the puck is 20 to 32 mm off at pictures 16 to 19 (flag is_aligned False). Run 5 reads 49 and 44 mm at pictures 8 and 9 with all flags good; the check's other tool puts the puck 8 to 14 mm outside the ink there (step length, finding 5). Of eight wordings tried only three give back fail, fail, pass: 8 cm with 12 mm, 8 cm with 17 mm, 10 cm with 17 mm. Four of five measured sheet-03 demonstrations pass at 12 mm (block_a_rule_on_demonstrations.out.txt). The leg after the first bend is about 10 cm long (EVIDENCE/step_length/02_top_view_run_10.png). The measure has no ruler check (plan section 3).
- The critic's fix: Replace the rule text by: 'A run keeps the line at the bend when the puck's centre is never more than 12 mm from the centre line on two pictures in a row, from the start to 8 cm of line past the bend's apex. The distance is taken to sheet 03's traced centre line (NOTES/Recording tool/2026-11-06_sheet_measurements.json, line_xy_cm), laid onto the taped sheet once per lay-out, so that "the bend" and "8 cm" are places on the line. A picture the scorer cannot measure is judged on the contact sheet (does the puck still touch the ink?) by a person who does not know the setting. Reported beside it: lost = more than 17 mm, and the largest distance in the window in mm.' Add to step 1 item 1: 'Before any rule is frozen the scorer must give back the check's verdicts on the logs of 11.11: runs 5 and 9 fail at the first bend, run 10 passes it, runs 2 and 4 are not lost.' Use the same wording for block D's 'first 10 cm'.
- Check: **stands**. [measured, code] EVIDENCE/stop_and_centring/run_measures.py and picture_measures.py measure the puck against a line map voted by the last 12 pictures. They hold no notion of a bend or of centimetres past it. [measured] EVIDENCE/stop_and_centring/robot_runs_steps.csv gives the critic's numbers back: run 10 reads 19.0 and 14.7 mm at pictures 4 and 5 (line followed back 17 mm) and 12.5 and 15.6 mm at pictures 21 and 22; run 5 reads 49.4 and 44.2 mm at pictures 8 and 9 with both flags good; run 9's 20 to 32 mm at pictures 16 to 19 carry the flag is_aligned False. [measured by me] I then tried the critic's fix as it is worded, which the critic had not done (its 8 cm was counted along the puck's own path). CHECK/fix_p1_on_the_three_runs.py lays sheet 03's traced line onto each run's own ink map (the step-length analyst's maps). The middle of the ink band lies a median 1.2 to 1.3 mm from the laid-on line. Against that line run 5 is 10 and 14 mm off at pictures 8 and 9 (so the scorer's 49 and 44 mm were wrong and unmarked), run 10 is 5 and 4 mm off at pictures 4 and 5 (the scorer's 19 and 15 mm were wrong), run 9 is 18 to 30 mm off at pictures 16 to 19 (the scorer's flagged readings were right, so leaving them out hides the loss). The rule gives fail, fail, pass for every window from 6 to 10 cm and for both 12 and 17 mm. Largest distance up to 8 cm past the apex: 40.7, 31.0 and 5.7 mm. It also works on short runs: built from the first 18 to 27 pictures only, the misfit is 1.5 to 1.6 mm and the puck's distances change by at most 2.3 mm (CHECK/fix_p1_on_short_runs.out.txt). One correction [calculated from the traced line, CHECK/sheet_03_bends.out.txt]: the first bend's apex lies 39 mm from the start cap, the second bend begins at 104 mm and has its apex at 127 mm. The straight leg between the bends is about 4 cm, not 10. The plan's 10 cm reaches past the second apex; the critic's 8 cm ends 0.8 cm before it. Neither measure has a ruler check, and both share the fitted camera.
- The checker's fix: Keep the critic's rule, with the places pinned as numbers: 'A run keeps the line at the first bend when the puck's centre is never more than 12 mm from the centre line on two pictures in a row, from the start until the puck has passed 119 mm of line from the start cap (8 cm past the bend's apex at 39 mm). The distance is taken to sheet 03's traced centre line (06_deliverables/research_notes/Recording tool/2026-11-06_sheet_measurements.json, line_xy_cm) laid onto each run's own pictures. A run whose ink band lies more than 2.5 mm (median) from the laid-on line is not scored by number. A picture without a puck reading is judged on the contact sheet (does the puck still touch the ink?) by someone who does not know the setting. Reported beside it: lost = more than 17 mm, and the largest distance in the window.' Step 1 item 1 gets the acceptance test: on the logs of 11.11 the scorer says fail, fail, pass for runs 5, 9 and 10 (the method does: CHECK/fix_p1_on_the_three_runs.out.txt) and does not call runs 2 and 4 lost (not yet tried). The same wording serves block D on sheet 02's traced line.

**P2** (high). Where: Step 2, block A: '4 short runs each', 'at least 3 of 4 passes where fixed 1.0 cm has at most 1 of 4', 'pass alike', 'the two best settings'

- Problem: Four runs per setting cannot carry this rule, and most outcomes are not covered by it. A setting that is no better can be taken on by luck. A setting that is really better is usually not taken on. If the old step passes 2 of 4, no rule applies. 'Pass alike' and 'the two best' are often undefined. The order is 'alternating', which a slow drift (tape, light, the operator getting quicker) can line up with.
- Evidence: [calculated] REVIEW/rule_probabilities.out.txt and rule_probabilities_more.out.txt. If every setting passes with the chance seen on 11.11 (1 of 3): one named new setting meets the rule with chance 0.066, at least one of the two new settings with 0.124; worst case 0.167 (pass chance 0.47). The 1 of 3 itself only says the true chance lies between 0.02 and 0.86. A real step from 1 in 3 to 2 in 3 is taken on with chance 0.35; from 0.25 to 0.75 with 0.55; from 0.2 to 0.8 with 0.67. The old step passes 2 or more of 4 with chance 0.41 at 1 in 3 and 0.69 at 1 in 2. Two equally good settings show different counts in 70 % of sessions; second and third place tie in 14 to 37 %. A rank rule on a measured number always gives an answer: with 5 runs each, 'rank sum of the new setting at most 19' is met by a no-better setting with chance 0.048 (12 of 252 orders) and takes on a setting that brings the largest offset from a median of 30 mm to 15 mm with chance 0.60 (pass counts: 0.29), to 10 mm with 0.91 (0.73) [simulation; the spread is a guess from the three runs of 11.11, which read 41, 33 and 16 mm].
- The critic's fix: Block A becomes: 'Model E, sheet 03, fixed 1.0 cm against fixed 0.65 cm, 5 short runs each, the order drawn by coin inside each pair. Number per run: the puck's largest distance from the centre line in the window of P1. Rule: 0.65 cm is taken on when its rank sum is at most 19; otherwise the step stays 1.0 cm. If fixed 1.0 cm keeps the line in 4 or 5 of 5 runs, write "with the controlled start the bend is not a step problem" and go on with 1.0 cm. The model's own length: 4 runs, reported; it replaces 0.65 cm only if its median is lower and none of its runs stalls or stops early; a tie goes to fixed 0.65 cm, the simpler rule.' Keep the sentence that anything closer needs more runs.
- Check: **partly**. The arithmetic is right [recalculated, CHECK/rules_recomputed.out.txt and count_rule_options.out.txt]. At a pass chance of 1 in 3 one named new setting meets the plan's rule with a chance of 0.066 and at least one of the two with 0.124 (worst case 0.167). A real step from 1 in 3 to 2 in 3 is taken on with 0.351. The old step passes 2 or more of 4, where no rule applies, with 0.41. The uncovered outcomes are real [measured, plan lines 163 and 169]. Three corrections. (1) The '1 of 3' mixes two models: model E lost the bend in 2 of 2 runs, the one pass was the team of five (plan 2.1). (2) The fix is weaker than claimed. The critic's 0.60 against 0.29 rests on a log-normal guess and compares with a stricter count rule than the plan's; the critic's own table gives the plan's rule 0.43 for the same case (REVIEW/rule_probabilities_more.out.txt). The numbers in hand fall into two groups instead: 5.7, 31.0 and 40.7 mm for the three runs (traced-line measure) and 4.4 to 6.1 mm with one of 40 mm for five demonstrations (REVIEW/block_a_rule_on_demonstrations.out.txt). With two groups the rank rule (12 of 252 orders = 0.048, confirmed) takes a real step on less often than the plan's own rule: 0.21 against 0.35 for 1 in 3 to 2 in 3, 0.48 against 0.67 for 0.2 to 0.8, 0.76 against 0.90 for 0.1 to 0.9 [simulated, 40,000 sessions; the two groups are my guess of the shape from those eight numbers]. The order inside a group is noise. (3) 'The own length replaces 0.65 cm only if its median is lower' is a coin toss: two equally good settings meet it in 47 to 54 % of sessions [simulated].
- The checker's fix: Block A: 'Model E, sheet 03, one taped lay-out, controlled start. Fixed 1.0 cm against fixed 0.65 cm, 5 pairs, the order inside each pair drawn by coin. A run keeps the line by the rule of P1. A run that ends before the window's end (stop, stall, refusal) counts as not kept and keeps its label. Rule: 0.65 cm is taken on when it keeps the line in at least 3 more runs than 1.0 cm; otherwise the step stays 1.0 cm.' This covers every outcome. [calculated] A setting that is no better is taken on with a chance of at most 0.055; a step from 0.2 to 0.8 with 0.68, from 0.1 to 0.9 with 0.93, from 1 in 3 to 2 in 3 with 0.30. The largest distance per run and the rank sum are reported, not decided on. 'The model's own length: 4 runs, reported (stalls, early stops, largest distance). It replaces 0.65 cm only if it keeps the line in all 4 runs while 0.65 cm loses it in 2 or more of 5' (met by luck with 0.01 to 0.11 [calculated]). Keep the plan's sentence that anything closer needs more runs.

[calculated] 3 runs: 0 of 3 still allows a true chance up to 0.63, 3 of 3 down to 0.37 (REVIEW/rule_probabilities.out.txt). **P4** (high). Where: Step 2, block E, and the order of the blocks; step 1 item 1

- Problem: The measure is tested last, in block E, but the rules of blocks A, C and D are decided with it in the same session. E as designed also cannot deliver. It tests only the stop place, not the distance from the line at a bend that A and D use. It needs 10 stops that the session will probably not produce. The photo is about as inexact as the 3 mm it should prove. After the lift the arm still hangs over the puck, so a person would lean over a held arm. The rule does not say whether all stops or ten of them must agree.
- Evidence: [measured, check] stop and centring, verifier's challenge 1: the ruler check 'should come before it is built in or used to accept and reject anything'; challenge 2: 'nobody should reach under a held arm ... measure after the lift with the arm clear'. [measured, code] the lift is 5 cm straight up (CODE/config.py, lift_at_end_m 0.05) and the tip stands 1.85 cm behind the puck's centre. [calculated] REVIEW/stops_for_block_e.out.txt: stops at a line's end can only come from the 20 full runs of B and C; 10 or more stops come with chance 0.05, 0.24 or 0.59 if 30, 40 or 50 % of them end at the end (11.11: 4 of 10, on trained sheets). [calculated] REVIEW/rule_probabilities_more.out.txt: the puck is 29 mm tall; with the phone tilted 6 degrees its top is drawn 3.0 mm beside its foot; 2.7 mm when the puck is 30 mm off the picture's centre with the lens 35 cm up.
- The critic's fix: Put the test of the measure into block 0, before any rule is used, as a test at known places: 'On a spare print of sheet 03 and one dashed print, thin light-grey circles of 24 mm are printed at about 20 places: on the line's middle, 7, 12 and 17 mm beside it, at the bend, at the line's end (centred, 5 mm short, 7 mm aside). The arm is placed by hand with the motors off as for a start; the driver saves one picture and the joints and moves nothing (a new "snapshot" option); the puck is set into each circle. Rule: the scorer is within 3 mm at 18 of 20 places and never more than 5 mm off. If not, nothing in this session is decided by the measure; block A is judged on contact sheets.' Keep a plain ruler reading at every real stop (millimetres of the line's end showing beyond or beside the puck), taken after the arm has driven back to the start pose.
- Check: **partly**. The core stands [measured]. The verifier's challenges 1 and 2 (stop and centring) say that the ruler check must come before the measure accepts or rejects anything and that nobody reaches under a held arm. Block E checks stop places only, while blocks A and D are judged by the distance from the line at a bend. lift_at_end_m is 0.05 and the tip stands 1.85 cm behind the puck's centre (CODE/config.py). The stop count is right for stops at a line's end: 10 or more from 20 runs with a chance of 0.05, 0.24 and 0.59 [recalculated]. The photo geometry is plain arithmetic and right. Two corrections. (1) 'Tested last' is too strong: block E takes its photos at every stop during the session; only the comparison comes afterwards, as do the rules. (2) The fix does not work as written [measured, code]. EVIDENCE/stop_and_centring/picture_measures.py needs at least two line votes per cell (minimum_line_votes 2) from the last pictures, so one snapshot gives no offset_mm at all; the first picture of runs 5 and 10 in robot_runs_steps.csv is empty for that reason. The line under the puck is hidden once the puck stands in its circle; in a run it was seen before the puck arrived. The flags and the sign use the tip's direction of travel, which a standing arm does not have. The test also needs a print of our own (sheet 03's shape with circles), so the critic's 'day 1 needs no new track prints' does not hold. That a hand must lean over a held arm for the photo is a guess; I could not check where the arm hangs above the puck.
- The checker's fix: Block 0 gets the test of the measure, built for the measure that decides (after P1 the distance to the laid-on centre line; for stops the end measure): 'One print of our own of sheet 03's shape with thin light-grey 24 mm circles at about 20 places: on the line's middle, 7, 12 and 17 mm beside it, at the first bend, at the line's end (centred, 5 mm short, 7 mm aside), two places beyond 40 cm reach and two with the forearm turned 60 degrees or more. At each place the arm is put by hand with the motors off about 3 to 6 cm behind the circle and then held. Two pictures of the bare line are saved, the puck is set into the circle, a third is saved (a new snapshot option; nothing moves). The scorer is given the print's own line direction as the direction of travel. First check: the grey circles are not read as ink. Pass: within 3 mm at 18 of 20 places and never more than 5 mm off. If not, no rule of this session is decided by number; block A is judged on contact sheets.' Plan 30 minutes for it, and the print with its 100 mm bar before day 1. Keep block E's rule (3 mm at 10 or more) but count the end of every run, the short runs of block A too: a phone photo from above, the puck in the photo's centre, the phone held level, taken while the arm waits above the start (see P6).

**P5** (high). Where: Step 1, item 2: 'a stall rule that ends a run whose last 15 pictures stay within 4 cm with less than 15 degrees of forearm turn (... fires in none of the 210 recordings [calculated])'

- Problem: The 'none of 210' was counted on the recorded steps, a median 1.05 cm long. Blocks A and B drive short steps. With the model's own length the same rule ends healthy runs. It would count against that setting in block A and cut runs short in block B, and no rule says how a stalled run counts.
- Evidence: [calculated; not a closed loop] REVIEW/stall_rule_with_short_steps.out.txt (earlier attempt) and REVIEW/stall_rule_detail.out.txt (recomputed, same numbers): the fold models' answers on the demonstrations of the sheet they never saw, chained along each demonstration. Own length: the rule ends 17 of 209 demonstrations (8.1 %) for the E recipe, 11 of them before the last tenth, for example a sheet-03 standard start at picture 35 of 110; 29 of 209 (13.9 %) for the A recipe. Fixed 0.65 cm: 0 and 1. Fixed 0.5 cm: 12 and 9. Recorded steps and fixed 1.0 cm: 0. A rule of 25 pictures, 4 cm, 15 degrees ends 0 of 209 (E) and 3 of 209 (A) at the own length and none at 0.65 or 0.5 cm; it still ends run 6 at picture 41 as driven and no other run of 11.11 (REVIEW/stall_rule_variants_on_the_ten_runs.out.txt). Today's rule in config.py (30 pictures, 3 cm) ends none of the 209 but never ends run 6 as driven.
- The critic's fix: Replace the sentence by: 'a stall rule of 25 pictures, 4 cm and 15 degrees for every setting (0 to 3 false endings in 209 demonstrations at short steps; run 6 ended at picture 41 [calculated]); a stalled run counts as not finished and is listed apart from lost runs.' Add the same count on the model's own answers to test_model_mode.py, so that a later change of the numbers is tested the same way.
- Check: **partly**. Right in direction, too small in size, and the fix does not hold. [measured] The plan's 'none of 210' is EVIDENCE/bends_and_two_ways/verify/v08_stall_rule_on_demonstrations.out.txt, counted on the recorded steps. [recalculated] The critic's chained count gives the same output when run again (CHECK/rerun_stall_rule_detail.out.txt): 17 and 29 of 209 at the model's own length, 0 and 1 at 0.65 cm. But that count takes one answer per demonstration picture, and a shorter step means more pictures per centimetre. [calculated by me, CHECK/stall_rule_paced_walk.out.txt and stall_rule_grid.out.txt] I walked each demonstration along its own path at the step the unseen-sheet model asked (a perfect follower; a second approximation that errs the other way from the critic's, not a robot run). The plan's rule then ends 110 (E recipe) and 132 (A recipe) of 209 walks at the own length, and 16 of 209 even at fixed 0.65 cm. The critic's rule of 25 pictures, 4 cm, 15 degrees ends 42 and 52 at the own length (40 of the 42 while the walker still made 1.5 cm or more inside the window) and none at 0.65 cm. Today's rule (30 pictures, 3 cm) ends 16 and 17. So the critic's '0 to 3 false endings' holds only inside its own method, and its rule would still count against the own length in block A, the fault it found in the plan. The cause is that the rule counts pictures: 15 pictures are 15 cm of path at 1 cm steps and 9.75 cm at 0.65 cm.
- The checker's fix: 'The stall rule counts path, not pictures: a run is ended as stalled when the pictures that hold the last 15 cm of the tip's own path (at least 15 pictures) stay within 4 cm, or when the last 40 pictures stay within 1.5 cm, each with less than 15 degrees of forearm turn. With 1 cm steps this is the rule the check proposed.' [calculated, CHECK/stall_rule_by_path.out.txt] It ends run 6 at picture 33 and no other run of 11.11; none of the 209 recordings; none of the demonstrations in the critic's chained count at any step; in the paced walk none at 0.65 or 0.5 cm and 4 (E) and 8 (A) of 209 at the own length, all where the model asks for almost no move for 40 pictures, which is a real hesitation. A stalled run counts as not finished and is listed apart from lost runs. The same three counts go into test_model_mode.py, so that a later change of the numbers is tested the same way.
