- Problem: 'Where it lay in the recordings' is not one place. The plan names no way to get tape marks onto the table. No code drives the real arm to a start pose. The item also hides further new movements (the drive back after a run, a second start pose for block D). As written, two people could tape two different lay-outs and both would be 'as recorded'. The plan asks for one checklist; it does not say what it must cover.
- Evidence: [measured] EVIDENCE/orientation_coverage/01_placements_per_episode.csv (the check's fitted lay-outs): sheet 03, end A, standard start, 8 episodes: the sheet's turn runs from 98.6 to 121.3 degrees (22.7 apart) and the line's start cap over 2.5 by 3.0 cm; sheet 04 end A: 23.5 degrees; sheet 05 end B: plans 05-01 and 05-02 (the lay-out of run 7) lie 32 degrees and 8 cm from the other four. Orientation coverage, verdict 3: the set-up picture plan_02-13.png draws the wrong end for the square lay-out of block D. The arm's pose is one place: tip (14.0, -1.5) cm, forearm straight, in 148 episodes; the puck stood 3.7 to 8.7 cm ahead (verdicts 4 and 5). [measured, code] CODE/model_mode.py: robot_run has only the hand-placed start; simulated_run goes to the start in one joint move, which on the real arm could sweep low over the sheet. CODE/record_episodes.py, __lower_to_start: 5 cm above the start first, then straight down, with the puck off the sheet; camera_shift_px checks the camera against a reference picture (warning at 4 px); a ghost of the reference picture guides the puck.
- The critic's fix: Rewrite item 3: 'Start poses have names in config.py (start mark: tip (14.0, -1.5) cm, forearm 0, wrist 13.5, hand -6.1 degrees). The driver goes there as the recording tool does: lift, travel 5 cm above, straight down, at the usual 20 % speed; it refuses unless the tip is at least 4 cm above the paper or the arm is parked. The puck stays off the sheet until the arm stands. One reference demonstration per sheet is named in the run list (those the runs of 11.11 aimed at: 02-01, 03-01, 04-01, 05-01, 06-01; for the square sheet 02 the recorded first picture of 02-13, not its set-up picture). The driver's own window lays that demonstration's first picture half-transparent over the live one: the sheet is slid until the lines match, taped, and the puck is set on its ghost. No second program is opened. Logged per run: the camera's shift (stop the session above 4 px), the lay-out fitted from the first pictures (void above 1 cm or 3 degrees), the start pose's name.' One checklist covers every new movement (drive to a start pose, drive back after a run, the scripted steps of block 0, the snapshot): each is tried in the simulator, then once on the robot without sheet and puck, with Emranul's go and the stop button in reach.
- Check: **partly**. The gaps are real [measured, code]. CODE/model_mode.py robot_run has only the hand-placed start; simulated_run goes to its start in one joint move. CODE/record_episodes.py: __lower_to_start goes 5 cm above first and then straight down, __check_camera_shift compares with a stored picture (warning above 4 px, config.py line 700), __with_reference_ghost lays the reference picture over the live one. The start is one arm pose [measured by me, frames.csv]: 149 episodes begin at the joints (-6.06, -41.87, -61.93, 0.0, 13.53, -6.15) degrees, tip (14.0, -1.5) cm. Size: the 22.7 degrees is the range of eight single fits whose own misfit is 0.7 to 1.6 cm (EVIDENCE/orientation_coverage/01_placements_per_episode.csv, column fit_median_cm). The verifier found two groups 11 to 11.5 degrees apart for sheet 03 and 7.5 to 8.5 for sheet 04, with a scatter of about 6 degrees in single values (step length, verdict 17). 'Not one place' stands; '23 degrees' is too large. The fix misses one fact [measured, D:/AI_Project/03_data/processed/v01_training_index/episodes.csv]: the camera moved on 8.11.26 at about 19:00. Every demonstration of sheets 01 to 03 and 29 of 45 of sheet 04 was recorded before, sheets 05 and 06 after. 02-01, 03-01, 04-01 and 02-13, which the fix names as references, are all from before. Against the stored reference the recording tool's own check read 14.2 to 28.3 px (median 14.9) on all 48 start-mark episodes after the move, and 1.5 to 8.1 px (median 5.3) on the standard starts before it. So 'stop the session above 4 px' against the stored reference stops at once, 4 px is inside the check's own noise, and a ghost from an old picture cannot match the block and the sheet together.
- The checker's fix: Item 3 as the critic rewrote it, with five changes. (1) The start is stored as the six recorded joint angles (-6.06, -41.87, -61.93, 0.0, 13.53, -6.15 degrees), not solved again from a tip point, so that the live picture and the ghost come from the same arm pose. (2) Reference pictures: for sheet 04 (plan 04-30 on), 05 and 06 a demonstration from after 8.11.26 19:00. For sheets 01 to 03 none exists, so the rule is written down: the sheet's line and the puck are matched to the ghost; the block at the bottom of the picture will sit about 15 px beside its ghost. (3) The camera check uses a new reference saved at the start pose at the beginning of day 1: warn above 4 px, stop above 10 px (largest reading without a move 8.3 px, smallest with the move 14.2 px). (4) After a run the arm lifts, drives to 5 cm above the start and waits there. Photo and ruler reading are taken, the puck is taken off the sheet, and only Enter lets the arm come down. (5) Before the first descent of each visit: the tape outline of the base unchanged, the descent stopped 3 cm up, the 5 mm gap under the block checked with a card. Per run the lay-out is fitted from the run's own pictures (the laid-on centre line of P1; void above 1 cm or 3 degrees from the block's first run). One checklist covers every new movement; each is tried in the simulator and once on the robot without sheet and puck, at 20 % speed, with Emranul's go and the stop button in reach.

**P7** (medium). Where: Step 2, block 0 ('a dry run without the puck'; 'commanded against measured tip move from the log')

- Problem: Block 0 names a measure but no pass rule, and the log cannot answer the question. The tip in the log is worked out from the joint angles the robot reports, so play in the joints, sag or a sticking puck are invisible. Without a puck the model's answers are arbitrary, so the run does not make the moves that matter (many steps of 1 to 3 mm in a row, twists in place). Whether the puck follows such small pushes is not tested. 'Does the controlled start work?' has no number either.
- Evidence: [measured] REVIEW/step_accuracy_from_the_logs.out.txt, 544 moves of 11.11: the reported tip ended a median 0.19 mm from the planned point, 0.31 mm at the 90th percentile, 0.60 mm at most. [calculated] that is 10 to 31 % of a step of 1 to 3 mm. [measured, check] twist and heading, 'what was missed' 6: 'nobody has seen whether the puck moves cleanly under such small pushes'; step length, challenge 2: this driver never commanded steps of 1 to 3 mm on the real arm (the recording tool did make such moves: 8 % of demonstrated steps are under 2 mm). [guess] in a twist in place joints 1 to 3 move while joint 4 turns; the tip may swing between start and end and nudge the puck; the log shows only start and end. [measured, code] the loop accepts any policy object (CODE/test_model_mode.py, HesitatingPolicy) and steps of 2 mm run through it on the simulated robot (REVIEW/driver_switches_without_robot.out.txt).
- The critic's fix: Block 0 becomes two short parts. '0a, no puck: a fixed list of answers goes through the normal loop (a stand-in policy): 10 steps of 1 mm, 10 of 2 mm, 10 of 3 mm straight ahead, then 5 twists in place of 5 degrees and 5 of 10 degrees. Pass: no refused step and no robot error; every step within 0.5 mm of its command in the log; the 60 mm total within 2 mm on a ruler lying beside the tip (phone photo before and after). 0b, the puck touching the tip on sheet 01: the same list. Pass: the puck's move per step, read from the pictures, within 1 mm of the tip's; a twist in place moves the puck less than 2 mm. Start pose: tip within 2 mm of (14.0, -1.5) cm and the first picture within 4 px of the stored one.' If 1 mm steps fail, the driver gets a smallest step (for example 2 mm) before block A.
- Check: **stands**. [measured, code] The tip in steps.csv is calculated from the joints the robot reports (model_mode.py, logged_row: magnet_tip_and_lean(state['joints'])), so play, sag or a sticking puck cannot show in the log. [measured] Block 0 has no pass number (plan line 162). The quotes are right: twist and heading, 'what was missed' 6, and step length, challenge 2. HesitatingPolicy in CODE/test_model_mode.py shows that the loop takes any policy object, and a 2 mm step runs on the simulated robot (CHECK/rerun_driver_switches_without_robot.out.txt). REVIEW/step_accuracy_from_the_logs.out.txt (544 moves: median 0.19 mm, 90th percentile 0.31 mm, largest 0.60 mm) was read, not run again. That a model with no puck in view gives arbitrary answers is a guess, and a fair one. One weakness in the fix: 'every step within 0.5 mm' lets a 1 mm step be half wrong, and 'the puck's move per step within 1 mm' is at the noise of the puck reading (two puck fits differ by a median 1.0 to 1.4 mm, stop and centring, verdict 1).
- The checker's fix: Block 0 as the critic's two parts (a fixed list of answers through the normal loop: 10 steps of 1 mm, 10 of 2 mm, 10 of 3 mm, 5 twists in place of 5 degrees, 5 of 10 degrees; first without the puck, then with the puck touching the tip on sheet 01), with the pass rule per group of ten steps: '0a: no refused step and no robot error; the logged tip moves 10, 20 and 30 mm, each within 10 %, and no step goes backwards; the 60 mm total within 2 mm on a ruler in a phone photo. 0b: the puck moves 10, 20 and 30 mm, each within 2 mm, read from the pictures and once from the ruler; the puck moves less than 2 mm in each group of five twists. Start pose: the six joints within 0.2 degrees of the stored ones.' If the 1 mm group fails, the driver gets a smallest step of 2 mm before block A. These runs are labelled tool tests.

**P8** (medium). Where: Step 2, heading ('about three hours, can be split in two') and last paragraph ('About 12 short and 24 full runs')

- Problem: Three hours covers the runs only if everything goes as on 11.11 and nothing is added. The plan adds longer runs (short steps), tape marks for eight lay-outs, photos, scoring block A before block B, and new movements. It does not say where the session is cut in two, although block B needs block A's result. It also makes block A wait for the print set, which it does not need.
- Evidence: [measured] REVIEW/run_times.out.txt and session_time.out.txt: on 11.11 a picture took 0.94 s; from the last picture of one run to the first of the next a median 181 s (108 to 275). Standard-start demonstrations need 114 pictures on sheet 03 and 107 and 83 on sheets 05 and 06 at about 1 cm per picture, so about 150 at 0.65 cm. [calculated] the plan's runs alone: 2.4 h with the shortest gap of 11.11, 3.2 h with the median, 4.2 h with the longest. [guess] on top: 15 min switching on and tests, 40 min tape for 8 lay-outs, 15 min scoring block A, 10 min checklists, 20 min repeated runs; four to five hours in all. Report section 7.4 assumed 1.86 min between runs; 11.11 measured 3.0. The scorer itself is quick: about 1.5 min for ten runs (EVIDENCE/stop_and_centring/run_order.txt). [calculated] the order proposed here: day 1 1.8 to 2.3 h (25 mostly short runs), day 2 2.4 to 2.8 h (21 runs), block C another 0.9 to 1.1 h (REVIEW/session_time_proposed.out.txt).
- The critic's fix: Plan two visits with a fixed cut. 'Day 1, about 2 hours, no new track prints needed: set-up and camera check; block 0 (P7) and the measure at known places (P4); sheet 01 three times as the known-good control (P11); block A (P2 and P3: 14 + 6 short runs). Score at the desk and choose the step. Day 2, about 2.5 hours: sheet 01 three times with the chosen step (the stop); sheet 05 seen against unseen (6 runs); two new tracks (6 runs); sheet 04 twice; then block D; then block C. If time runs out: cut C first, then D, then the own-length runs. Never cut block 0, the sheet-01 control or the unseen-model runs.' Write the planned minutes beside each block in the checklist.
- Check: **partly**. [recalculated from REVIEW/session_time.out.txt] The plan's runs alone come to 191 minutes at the median gap between runs of 11.11 (181 s), 145 at the shortest gap and 251 at the longest. So the plan's 'about three hours' is the runs alone at the pace of 11.11: not far out, but nothing else fits in. The four to five hours are a guess, and may be too high: the controlled start saves the placing by hand that was part of the 181 s. Two points stand as written [measured, plan lines 155 and 235]: no cut is named, and the calendar makes all of session 2 wait for the prints although blocks 0 and A use sheets 01 and 03 only. The 1.86 minutes of report 7.4 is quoted correctly. One point in the fix does not hold: day 1 is said to need no new prints, but the critic's own P4 puts the measure test on 'a spare print of sheet 03 and one dashed print' with circles. That needs the print tool for a given centre line, the PDF and the printer check. The 45 s per place are a guess; with two bare pictures and one with the puck I would plan 30 minutes [guess].
- The checker's fix: 'Two visits with a fixed cut. Day 1, about 2.5 hours, needs one print of our own (sheet 03's shape with circles) and no new tracks: set-up, table and camera check; block 0; the measure at known places; sheet 01 three times as the known-good control; block A (10 + 4 runs with model E, 6 with the unseen-sheet model). Scoring at the desk; the step is chosen. Day 2, about 3 hours: sheet 01 three times with the chosen step; sheet 05 with 1.0 cm, with the chosen step and with the unseen-sheet model (8 runs); our own print of sheet 01 twice (print control); two new tracks (6 runs); sheet 04 twice; block D; block C.' If time runs out: cut C first, then the sheet-04 repeat, then D, then the own-length runs. Never cut block 0, the sheet-01 control, the 1.0 cm runs on sheet 05 or the unseen-model runs. The checklist carries the planned minutes beside each block.

**P9** (medium). Where: Step 2, block A ('ended with Esc after the bend') and every full run of blocks B and C

- Problem: A person who knows the setting decides when a short run ends. Nothing says when a full run may be ended by hand, which runs are void and repeated, or how a run counts that stops early, stalls or is refused by a guard. The earlier protocol had these rules; the plan dropped them. With one operator who also places the puck and scores, this is the place where a wish can leak into the result.
- Evidence: [measured] NOTES/Model and training/2026-11-09_training_set_up_analysis.md, section 7.4: 'The operator ends a run early only when the puck has left the paper, the arm has not moved for 5 steps, or for safety'; a void list; a warm-up run; two roles. NOTES/Model and training/2026-11-11_first_robot_session_checklist.md, known limits: Esc is read once per step. On 11.11 pucks came back after leaving the ink: run 4 from 20.9 mm to 1.0 mm in six pictures (stop and centring, verdict 10), runs 5 and 9 back onto the ink at pictures 13 to 15 (step length, finding 5); an early Esc would have cut these. 9 of the 10 runs ended with a stop of their own, five of them away from the line's end (REVIEW/run_times.out.txt). [measured, code] --step-budget exists; a travel budget does not.
- The critic's fix: Add under the table of step 2: 'Short runs end by themselves: a tip-travel budget of 22 cm (new switch); until it exists --step-budget 22, 34 and 45 for 1.0 cm, 0.65 cm and the own length. Full runs are ended by hand only when the puck has left the paper, or for safety; the stall rule and the budget of 400 end the rest. Every run gets one of: finished, lost, stopped early, never stopped, stalled, refused, void. Void (repeated at the end of the block) only for a lost connection, a set-up mistake, or a person or object in the camera's view. Whoever scores from the contact sheets does not see the run list.'
- Check: **stands**. [measured] The plan has no rule for ending a full run by hand, no void list and no labels (lines 155 to 170). The earlier protocol had all of them (2026-11-09_training_set_up_analysis.md, section 7.4: 'The operator ends a run early only when the puck has left the paper, the arm has not moved for 5 steps, or for safety', the void list, the warm-up run, two roles). The checklist of 11.11 says Esc is read once per step (line 116). The come-backs are right: run 4 from 20.9 to 1.0 mm (stop and centring, verdict 10), run 5 back on the ink at pictures 14 to 15 (step length, verdict 2). --step-budget exists, a travel budget does not (model_mode.py, main). One number in the fix is too tight [measured by me, CHECK/travel_needed_for_short_runs.out.txt]: until the puck is 8 cm past the apex the eight standard-start demonstrations of sheet 03 needed 16.2 to 21.3 cm of tip travel (median 18.7), and run 10 would have needed about 20.7 cm from the recorded start. A budget of 22 cm leaves 0.7 cm, so a run that swings a little wide ends before its window is complete.
- The checker's fix: Under the table of step 2: 'Short runs end by themselves at a tip-travel budget of 27 cm (new switch); until it exists --step-budget 27 for 1.0 cm and 42 for 0.65 cm. A run whose puck has not passed the window's end by then is scored on the part it covered and marked window not completed. Full runs are ended by hand only when the puck has left the paper, or for safety; the stall rule and the budget of 400 end the rest. Every run gets one label: finished, lost, stopped early, never stopped, stalled, refused, void. Void, and repeated at the end of its block, only for a lost connection, no fresh picture, a set-up mistake, or a person or object in the camera's view. Contact sheets are judged by someone who does not see the run list.'

**P10** (medium). Where: Step 1, item 4 (the print set); section 2.4 ('They must be drawn again')

- Problem: make_test_tracks.py cannot make this print set by changing numbers. It draws only its own random curves, writes SVG only, has one dash pattern and one line style per track, and its rule cannot draw a bend as tight as the course sheets. The sealed set does not belong in the zip for session 2. Nothing checks that a traced print matches the real sheet or that the printer prints at true size.
- Evidence: [measured, code] CODE/make_test_tracks.py: no way in for a given centre line (track_svg would take one); SVG only (cairosvg and svglib are not installed; reportlab 5.1 and matplotlib are); dash 35 mm and gap 15 mm (sheet 06: 20 and 10); a track is either solid or dashed; pieces of constant bend are 30 to 140 mm long. [calculated] at a radius of 10 mm one piece turns 172 to 802 degrees. [measured] REVIEW/track_rule_with_tight_bends.out.txt: radius limit 10 mm: 0 of 6 tracks kept; 13 mm: 3 of 6 with tightest bends of 14.7, 22.1 and 22.8 mm. REVIEW/course_sheets_under_the_track_rule.out.txt: by the generator's own measure sheets 03 to 06 bend to 6, 6, 20 and 10 mm and the rule refuses all four. REVIEW/track_rule_with_corner_pieces.out.txt: with a second kind of piece (radius 8 to 13 mm, turning 60 to 120 degrees, at least two per track) 6 of 6 tracks are kept in 1 s, tightest bends 8.5 to 10.6 mm, the gap rule unchanged. Traced centre lines exist twice: NOTES/Recording tool/2026-11-06_sheet_measurements.json (line_xy_cm, all six sheets, in cm on the paper, traced from the course's photos; sheet 05's sideways place is given to plus or minus 0.3 cm) and EVIDENCE/dashed_line/reference_line_05.csv and reference_line_06.csv (in the robot's frame of runs 7 and 8; the first 2 to 3 cm under the puck are missing: 53.8 against 56.6 cm for sheet 05).
- The critic's fix: Rewrite item 4 as work, not as a rerun: '(a) corner pieces in the rule, as tested; (b) a function that draws a given centre line solid and dashed 20/10 from sheet_measurements.json; (c) every new track written twice, solid and dashed; (d) PDF at true size with reportlab, plus one page with a 100 mm bar to check the printer before anything else is printed; (e) each traced print is laid over the real sheet 05 or 06 against a window: the lines agree to 2 mm or the print is not used; (f) tracks.json rebuilt from the files.' The sealed set is not generated now: only its rule and seed are committed; the other student prints it in the final week.
- Check: **stands**. [measured, code] CODE/make_test_tracks.py: drawn_track makes only its own random curves; track_svg writes SVG and would take any curve; one dash pattern from config.py (35 and 15 mm; sheet 06 has 20 and 10); each track is solid or dashed by its plan; pieces of constant bend are 30 to 140 mm long, which at a radius of 10 mm is 172 to 802 degrees [calculated]. cairosvg and svglib are not installed; reportlab 5.1.0 and matplotlib are; PyMuPDF is installed too [measured]. The critic's outputs on tight bends and corner pieces were read, not run again. The traced lines exist in both places named; sheet 05's camera-made line is 53.8 cm against 56.6 cm (EVIDENCE/dashed_line/01_sheet_geometry.out.txt). Size: printing each track solid and dashed and changing the dash lengths are two lines and two numbers; the real work is the given centre line, the corners and the PDF. One point in the fix is too strict [measured by me, CHECK/traced_against_camera_line.out.txt]: the photo-traced line and the line measured from the robot's own pictures agree in shape to a median 0.9 mm on sheet 05 (largest 3.4 mm) and 1.3 mm on sheet 06 (90th percentile 2.9 mm, largest 7.4 mm, on the dashes). A hard 2 mm gate may throw out a print that is good enough for its question.
- The checker's fix: Item 4 as the critic's work list (a) to (f), with three changes. For (e): 'Each traced print is laid over the real sheet against a window: the lines agree within 3 mm everywhere; where they differ by more than 2 mm the place is noted on the print's record.' The circle print of sheet 03 for the measure test (P4) is made first, together with the 100 mm bar page. The sealed set is not drawn now and its seed is not committed: Marshal picks the seed in the final week and prints the set, because a committed seed lets anyone redraw it. For (d), PyMuPDF may turn the SVG into PDF in a few lines (from memory, not checked); the 100 mm bar decides whichever way is used.

**P11** (medium). Where: Step 2 as a whole (the list of blocks 0 to E)

- Problem: Three controls are missing. No run shows that what worked on 11.11 still works from the new start, which the model has never driven on the robot. Nothing measures the stop under the new step on a line the model can finish, although the plan itself expects the stop to come earlier and Emranul wants the end covered. Sheet 04, the strongest failure of 11.11, is not driven again.
- Evidence: [measured, plan] section 2.2 item 1: the model's own length 'makes the stop come about 0.8 cm earlier, about 5 mm short at the median' [calculated there]. Block A's runs end before the line's end and block B's lines are the ones most runs will not finish, so few stops will exist. [measured, check] stop and centring, 'what was missed' 1: 'the stop tests must be repeated after any change of the step rule'; 5: 'there is no repeat of any run, so nothing is known about the scatter from run to run'. Twist and heading, 'what was missed' 5: sheet 04 'has no remedy in the plan ... a repeat with the standard placement and today's driver comes before any conclusion' (its sheet lay 15 to 25 degrees off and the start 5 cm off). All ten runs of 11.11 began with the block 2.3 to 3.6 cm from the puck; the recordings begin 5.5 cm back (plan 2.2 item 3). [calculated] the 8 runs below take 31 to 39 min (REVIEW/session_time_proposed.out.txt).
- The critic's fix: Add three small blocks. 'Control: model E on sheet 01 from the controlled start with the driver of 11.11, 3 runs, before block A; if fewer than 2 finish, stop and repair the start.' 'Stop: the same 3 runs again with the step block A chose; compare where the puck stops with the 3 control runs (ruler reading and scorer); if the median is more than 3 mm shorter, the end push is looked at before session 3.' 'Sheet 04: model E, driver of 11.11, sheet taped as in plan 04-01, 2 runs, labelled a repeat, no rule.' These 8 runs take the place of block C's 8.
- Check: **stands**. [measured] All ten runs of 11.11 began with the block 2.3 to 3.6 cm from the puck where the recordings begin 5.5 cm back (orientation coverage, verdict 5). So model E has never driven from the recorded start on the robot, and a known-good control is the cheapest way to tell a broken start from a broken step. The quotes are right: stop and centring, 'what was missed' 1 ('The stop tests must be repeated after any change of the step rule') and 5; twist and heading, 'what was missed' 5 (sheet 04). Block A's runs end before the line's end, so the plan as written produces almost no stop under the new step, although it expects the stop to move (plan 2.2 item 1). One number in the fix is weak [simulated by me, CHECK/stop_comparison_rule.out.txt; normal scatter of 2.8 to 4.2 mm is a guess from the four robot stops and the demonstrations' ends]: with 3 against 3 runs, 'the median is more than 3 mm shorter' fires by luck in 13 to 23 % of sessions and catches the 3.5 mm that a 0.65 cm step takes off the last move in 55 to 58 %. Since it only says 'look at the end push', it can stay.
- The checker's fix: The critic's three small blocks, unchanged in size: 'Control: model E on sheet 01 from the controlled start with the driver of 11.11, 3 runs, before block A; if fewer than 2 finish, stop and repair the start. Stop: the same 3 runs with the step block A chose; the stop places are reported side by side (ruler and scorer); if the median is more than 3 mm shorter, the end push is looked at before session 3. Nothing about the end push is decided on these six runs (the trigger fires by luck in about one session in five). Sheet 04: model E, driver of 11.11, one taped lay-out, 2 runs, labelled a repeat, no rule; it is the first to go after block C.'

**P12** (medium). Where: Step 2, block C

- Problem: The rule can name the wrong cause, the runs are split 3, 3, 1, 1, and no decision in section 6 waits for the answer. Model E learnt both shapes together with their look, so a third outcome is likely: it fails on any print that looks different from the one it learnt. The rule would then say 'shape'. The two new prints are ours and the two repeats are the course's, so the print and the tracing are mixed in as well. 'Fails like run 8' is not a number. The measure is weakest on dashes.
- Evidence: [calculated] REVIEW/rule_probabilities.out.txt: if dashes do nothing and every print is finished with chance 0.5, the rule says 'dashes' with chance 0.25 (0.17 at 0.3 or 0.7); one repeat run of sheet 05 fails by chance 30 % of the time even at a true rate of 0.7. REVIEW/rule_probabilities_more.out.txt: if E finishes a learnt print with chance 0.8 and any changed print with 0.2, the rule says 'shape' with chance 0.90 and 'dashes' with 0.09; both would be wrong. [measured, check] dashed line, 'what was missed' 8: E's manoeuvres 'are tied to the look and shape of the sheet they were learned on'. Plan section 3: the measure 'is unreliable on the dashed sheet'. Decisions 1 to 8 of section 6 do not mention dashes; step 3 already prints every new track solid and dashed and step 4 already tests the dash augmentation.
- The critic's fix: Mark block C 'first to be cut'. If it is run, spend the same 8 runs as 2 per cell: 'shape 05 solid, shape 05 dashed, shape 06 solid, shape 06 dashed, all four printed by us from the same tracing, order mixed. Dashes are judged on 4 dashed against 4 solid runs ("dashes matter" when solid finishes at least 3 of 4 and dashed at most 1 of 4: chance 0.10 if dashes do nothing, 0.67 if they cut finishing from 0.8 to 0.2 [calculated]); shape on 4 against 4. Runs are scored against the print's own centre line, not against the ink.' Write beside it what the answer changes: 'if dashes matter, the dash-cutting augmentation is the first candidate of step 4.'
- Check: **stands**. [recalculated, CHECK/rules_recomputed.out.txt] If dashes do nothing, the plan's rule says 'dashes' with a chance of 0.25 at a finish chance of 0.5 (0.17 at 0.3 or 0.7). If model E finishes only the prints it learnt (0.8 against 0.2), the rule says 'shape' with 0.90. [measured] That third truth is the check's own (dashed line: dashes cut into run 7's pictures take away 62 to 70 % of the twist in the walk; 'what was missed' 2). No decision in section 6 names dashes; step 3 prints every new track solid and dashed and step 4 tests the dash augmentation, so block C changes little. 'Fails like run 8' is not a number. The critic's 2 by 2 lay-out is sound (0.10 and 0.67, recalculated), and the tracings it prints from are good enough: they agree with the robot's own view of the lines to a median 0.9 and 1.3 mm (CHECK/traced_against_camera_line.out.txt). The fix does not name the reading that its own third truth needs.
- The checker's fix: Block C is marked first to be cut. If it is run: 'shape 05 solid, shape 05 dashed, shape 06 solid, shape 06 dashed, all four printed by us from the same tracings, 2 runs each, order mixed, driver of 11.11, scored against the print's own centre line. Three readings, each 4 runs against 4: dashes (solid against dashed), shape (05 against 06), learnt by heart (the two prints model E knows, 05 solid and 06 dashed, against the two it does not). A reading counts when one side finishes at least 3 of 4 and the other at most 1 of 4 (met by luck with 0.10, by a real 0.8 against 0.2 with 0.67).' Written beside it: 'if dashes count, the dash-cutting augmentation is the first candidate of step 4; if learnt by heart counts, new loop shapes come first in recording round 2.'

**P13** (medium). Where: Step 2, block D; decision 5 in section 6

- Problem: Two runs per start cannot choose a start. The rule leaves a tie, 'both keep it' and '2 against 1' open. Model E is the wrong judge: it was trained on six episodes of this lay-out that begin a third way, and never on the turned start as a start. What E does says little about a model retrained on the new start, yet decision 5 fixes the start 'for every later run and recording'. The turned start is hard to place by hand twice the same, and '5 cm behind the puck' with a straight forearm can mean towards the base or along the line.
- Evidence: [calculated] REVIEW/rule_probabilities.out.txt: two equally good starts show different counts in 58 to 63 % of sessions; a start that works 8 times in 10 against one that works 2 in 10 leads strictly in 82 %. [measured, check] orientation coverage: 148 of 209 episodes start with the forearm straight; the six square-portrait episodes go round the puck in at least four ways and score 0.62 to 0.72 offline (verdicts 1, 4, 11). EVIDENCE/orientation_coverage/verify/v16_pre_turned_start_pose.out.txt: the turned start needs the tip at (16.5, -5.9) cm and joint 4 at -90.5 degrees (rod lean 14 degrees; 85 pictures in 22 episodes lie near that pose, none as a start). Hand-placed starts on 11.11 lay 1.3 to 5.0 cm and up to 10 degrees off (plan 2.2 item 3).
- The critic's fix: Reword block D: 'A first look, no decision. Both starts are named start poses the arm drives to (straight: the start mark; turned: tip (16.5, -5.9) cm, joint 4 -90.5 degrees, for the taped square lay-out). 2 runs each, ended by the travel budget after 12 cm. Reported: the puck's largest distance from the centre line in the first 10 cm.' Decision 5 moves to step 3: the start is chosen in the hand trial and confirmed after retraining, on lines the model has not seen. Cut D second if time runs out.
- Check: **stands**. [recalculated] With 2 runs each, two equally good starts show different counts in 58 to 63 % of sessions. [measured] Orientation coverage: the six square-portrait episodes go round the puck in at least four ways and score 0.62 to 0.72 offline (verdict 11, 'what was missed' 2); none of the 209 episodes starts with the forearm turned 40 degrees or more within 25 cm of the base ('what was missed' 4); verify/v16_pre_turned_start_pose.out.txt gives tip (16.5, -5.9) cm, joint 4 -90.5 degrees, rod lean 14.0, 85 pictures in 22 episodes. The plan is also at odds with itself: step 3 rule 1 already writes the turned start down as the one start (line 181), while decision 5 waits for block D (line 250). The fix is right.
- The checker's fix: Block D: 'A first look, no decision. Both starts are named start poses the arm drives to (straight: the recorded start joints; turned: tip (16.5, -5.9) cm, joint 4 -90.5 degrees, for the taped square lay-out). 2 runs each, ended by the travel budget. Reported: the puck's largest distance from sheet 02's laid-on centre line in the first 10 cm.' Decision 5 moves to step 3: the start is chosen in the hand trial and confirmed after retraining, on lines the model has not seen. Cut D after block C and the sheet-04 repeat.

**P14** (medium). Where: Step 1, item 2 (driver switches); step 0 ('nothing has been committed since 10.11'); decision 3 ('any time')

- Problem: The switch list understates the new work and leaves out the bookkeeping that some 40 runs with different models and settings need. The run log does not say which sheet, start or code version a run used. The driver is an untracked file on one laptop. A wrong model of the same recipe loads without any warning. 'Rules fixed in advance' cannot be shown while nothing is committed.

Only in config.py: step_m 0.01, the stall numbers (30 pictures, 3 cm, 15 degrees), step budget 250, executor check from 0.5 cm. The loop already accepts step_m 0.0065 or 0.002 and the stall numbers as overrides (run on the simulated robot with the model without sheet 03). ModelPolicy checks only that the files exist and fit the recipe; model E and its fold models share one recipe. The first run of 11.11 was labelled 'check_no_puck' and had a puck (EVIDENCE/brief.md). The checklist of 11.11 has command lines with up to five long paths. git status: 02_code/model_mode.py, test_model_mode.py and make_test_tracks.py are untracked; checkpoints are ignored by git (.gitignore line 8).

The scorer refuses a run whose log does not match the list.' Change decision 3 to 'before session 2': plan, rules, run list, driver and scorer are committed and pushed first, so that 'fixed in advance' can be shown and Marshal can read them. Copy 03_data/model_runs to a second disk after each day.

[measured, code] model_mode.py main has --label, --run-name, --checkpoint, --metrics, --twist-gain, --step-length, --length-gain and --step-budget, and nothing for the fixed step's length, the stall numbers, a start pose or a travel budget. summary.json holds label, ending, model files and parameters, not the sheet, the start or the code version. ModelPolicy only checks that the files exist and loads them into the recipe; model E and its fold models share one recipe, so a wrong file of that recipe loads without a word. The earlier protocol asked for exactly this ('A written plan, committed to git before the first trial', report 7.4). One caution on the fix: the plan says nothing was committed on Emranul's instruction, so 'before session 2' is his decision to take, not a change the planner can write in.
- The checker's fix: Step 1 gets the critic's run list and wrapper (run number, block, sheet, lay-out, start pose, model key, step setting, budget; shown in the window before Enter; written into summary.json with the code commit and checksums of model_mode.py and config.py; the scorer refuses a run that does not match the list). Push now so that Marshal can read them, or commit on this laptop only and push later?' 03_data/model_runs is copied to a second disk after each day.

New tracks begin with a straight piece of 3 to 6 cm, so that sheet 01's start picture serves as their ghost (line and puck matched, see P6). The two tracks of block B are named in the run list: solid, two corners or more, chosen by the seed. One line more for the checklist: for every new lay-out the paper's far edge and the arm's reach stay clear of the table edge, measured before taping.

### Missing from the plan, by the critic

1. What is in the camera's view and who stands where: the check found that the blue hoodie may have made run 4's stop (stop chance 0.90, 0.22 to 0.65 with it painted over; plan 2.1). The session needs a line in the checklist (nothing blue or dark in view, people behind the robot), the brightness of each run's first picture logged, and the time of day noted (11.11 was driven from 17:20 under the ceiling light).
2. A camera check with a stop rule at the start of each visit: the first picture at the start pose against a stored reference picture, stop above 4 px (the recording tool's own limit, config.py camera_shift_warning_px). The plan has this only as a line in the risk table. The scorer also needs the camera's picture shift fitted for the new day (EVIDENCE/stop_and_centring/camera_shift.py has the runs of 11.11 written into it).
3. Roles. With one person the same hands place the puck, press Enter and Esc, watch the stop button, read the ruler and score. The earlier protocol (report 7.4) had two roles and a scorer who does not know the setting. Marshal has seen none of this work (nothing is committed).
4. Which copy of the unseen-sheet model is driven. Two seeds exist (run names resnet18_chunk_stop_weight_25 and resnet18_chunk_stop_weight_25_seed_2) and they answer differently, for example on picture 20 of run 5: forward +0.28 against -0.09 cm (REVIEW/fold_model_loads.out.txt) [measured]. Name the seed in the run list, or drive both.
5. A print control: one print of our own of a sheet the model knows (for example sheet 02), driven twice by model E, to tell 'our printer and paper' from 'a new shape'. Without it a failure on a new track or on the solid print of shape 06 has two explanations.
6. What each block's answer changes. Only A (decision 6) and D (decision 5) are tied to a decision; B and C are not. A block whose answer changes nothing should not use robot time.
7. The cut between the two visits and the fact that the first visit needs no new track prints, so it need not wait for the print set (blocks 0 and A use sheets 01 and 03 only).
8. A check that the robot and the table are where they were when the table plane was taught (6.11): the driver takes the pushing height from the taught plane, and the new start move comes down to 5 mm above the paper by itself. One line in the checklist: tape outline of the base unchanged, else teach again first.
9. How runs are scored on dashed prints and at crossings, where the measure fails (plan section 3; stop and centring, 'what was missed' 6). For our own prints the answer is the print's own centre line; the plan does not say so.
10. A backup of the run logs and of the model files: 03_data is not in git, the checkpoints are ignored by git and exist on this laptop and on ki-014 only.
11. One uncounted warm-up run at the start of each visit and a written list of what makes a run void (both were in report 7.4).
12. A test that the puck is not nudged when the forearm twists in place and that it follows pushes of 1 to 3 mm; block 0 is driven without the puck (see P7).

### The same list, checked

1. 1 (what is in the camera's view): really missing; the plan names the hoodie only in 2.1 and 2.3. It matters for stops (run 4's stop chance fell from 0.90 to 0.22 to 0.65 with the hoodie painted over). A checklist line and the brightness of each first picture cost nothing; report 7.4 already had the limits 0.67 to 1.12.
2. 2 (camera check with a stop rule): half missing; the plan has it only as a line in the risk table. It matters, but the critic's version must change: the stored reference is from before the camera moved and reads 14.2 px or more today, and readings up to 8.3 px occurred without any move [measured, episodes.csv]. Save a new reference on day 1; warn above 4 px, stop above 10 px. camera_shift.py does have the runs of 11.11 written into it [measured].
3. 3 (roles): really missing; report 7.4 had two roles. It matters less once scoring is a fixed command; it still matters for who may press Esc and who judges the contact sheets.
4. 4 (which seed of the unseen-sheet model): not really missing. Plan 2.2 item 5 names the folder 2026-11-10_resnet18_chunk_stop_weight_25, the first seed. The two seeds do answer differently (+0.28 against -0.09 cm on picture 20 of run 5, confirmed by a rerun). Repeating the folder name in the run list is enough.
5. 5 (print control): really missing, and it matters more than its place in the list suggests. Step 3 plans about 48 episodes on our own prints, and no run has shown that our printer's black and our paper look like the course's to the camera. Two runs of model E on our own print of sheet 01 answer that in under ten minutes [guess of the time].
6. 6 (what each block's answer changes): right. Only A and D are tied to decisions 6 and 5; B has no rule and C no decision [measured, plan section 6]. It matters: a block without a consequence should not use robot time.
7. 7 (the cut between the visits): right about the cut. Not quite right about the prints: the first visit needs one print of our own for the measure test, by the critic's own P4.
8. 8 (robot and table where the plane was taught): really missing, and it matters for safety. The new start move comes down to 5 mm above the paper by itself (pusher_clearance_above_sheet_m 0.005), where on 11.11 a person set the height and placed_point_problem checked it. Add a first descent that stops 3 cm up and a card under the block.
9. 9 (scoring on dashed prints and at crossings): really missing; plan section 3 calls the measure unreliable on the dashed sheet. The laid-on centre line of P1 answers it for every print whose line we know; the dashed-line analysis already did this for sheets 05 and 06 (EVIDENCE/dashed_line/registrations.csv).
10. 10 (backup of logs and models): right; 03_data and the checkpoints are outside git (.gitignore lines 7 to 9). It matters; a copy after each day is enough.
11. 11 (warm-up run and void list): right, both were in report 7.4. The void list matters and belongs in P9's fix; block 0 can serve as the warm-up.
12. 12 (puck under twists in place and pushes of 1 to 3 mm): right, and the same point as P7; block 0b settles it.

### Missed by the critic

1. The step rule is never tried on the loop against a baseline of the same day. The verified results warn that the one loop that worked relied on the stretched steps (run 7's walk: 2.3 cm asked over nine steps, 9 cm driven) and ask to test the new step 'on sheet 05 first, on the same day as a fixed-step baseline' (EVIDENCE/check_results_complete.md, dashed line, verifier's challenge 2). Neither the plan's blocks nor the critic's lean order do that, so decision 6 would rest on one bend of sheet 03. Fix: on day 2 model E drives sheet 05 with 1.0 cm and with the chosen step, 3 runs each, alternating; a step that finishes 2 or more fewer loops is not adopted as the only step.
2. The camera moved on 8.11.26, and 'a start like the recordings' was never defined for that. Every demonstration of sheets 01 to 03 and 29 of 45 of sheet 04 was recorded before the move; the stored reference picture too [measured, episodes.csv: 14.2 to 28.3 px on all 48 later start-mark episodes]. The same place on the table and the same picture now differ by about 15 px. Fix: write down that the sheet's line and the puck are matched to the old picture, save a new camera reference on day 1, and take references for sheets 04 to 06 from after the move.
3. The stall rule is written in pictures, so it means something different at every step length; the critic swapped one picture count for another. In a paced walk the plan's rule ends 16 of 209 healthy walks even at fixed 0.65 cm, the control setting, and the critic's rule ends 42 to 52 at the model's own length [calculated, CHECK/stall_rule_grid.out.txt]. Fix: count path (the last 15 cm of the tip's own path within 4 cm, or 40 pictures within 1.5 cm), see P5.
4. Session 2 has no run on a lay-out that was never recorded, although plan section 3 measures 'works however the sheet lies' exactly there, and block D mixes the start question with sheet 02's arc and its six demonstrations that disagree. Fix: in block D use sheet 01, a line model E finishes, taped at about 45 degrees in a lay-out that was never recorded: 2 runs from the straight start pose and 2 from a pre-turned pose. Then the only new thing is the orientation. Still a first look, no decision.
5. Two of the critic's own fixes collide on the robot. P4 takes the photo 'after the arm has driven back to the start pose'; P6 keeps the puck off the sheet until the arm stands. When the puck stopped near the start (short runs, a stall, sheet 06 whose ends lie 11 cm apart) the arm would come down beside or onto a puck that must stay for the photo. Fix: after a run the arm drives to 5 cm above the start and waits; photo and ruler, puck off, then Enter lets it come down.

### To keep

1. A control beside every question and the rule written down before the session. The rules need repair, the principle must stay.
2. The fixed 0.65 cm step beside the model's own length. Without it a win of 'own length' cannot be told from a win of 'shorter steps' (check, step length, 'what was missed' 4).
3. Driving a model that never saw the sheet. The files exist and load, and the whole loop runs with them on the simulated robot [measured: REVIEW/fold_model_loads.out.txt, driver_switches_without_robot.out.txt].
4. The controlled start as the normal start, with the sheet taped to marks, and starts placed by hand kept as a separate, labelled test.
5. A dry run before any run with the puck, and small steps checked on the arm before step settings are compared.
6. Short runs at one named bend for the driver question, with the settings mixed in order rather than one after the other.
7. The scorer as a separate command on the run's folder, kept out of the driver, with a sheet of small pictures saved beside every number.
8. 'No pass rule: this is the first measure of the exam case', and the sentence that four runs per setting can only show large differences. Both are honest and should not be softened.
9. A stall rule and a step budget of 400 so that no run hangs; only the stall numbers must change (P5).
10. The new movement gets its own checklist and Emranul's go, and nothing runs on the robot without it (plan, first paragraph and step 1 item 3).
11. Section 8: no new hand-written steering, no twist factor, no switch of model as the fix, and the stop rule left alone while the step is 1 cm.
12. Every new track printed once solid and once dashed. It unties dashes from shape for good, which block C cannot do for the course sheets.
13. The 12 mm and the 7 mm stop rule reported side by side, and the stop over whole episodes reported next to every score (step 1 item 5).
14. The ruler or photo check of the measure as an idea: keep it, but run it first and at known places (P4).
15. Allowing the robot work to be split over two visits.

## Does the plan aim at the exam, at what Emranul asked for, and fit the calendar? (folder `exam_and_schedule`)

**The critic's verdict.** Partly sound. The direction is right: drive the exam case on the robot, record more different tracks in one written style, fix rules before each session, judge on shapes the model has not trained on. Three changes matter most. (1) The plan names 'does it stop at all on an unseen line' as the bigger exam risk (right stop in 106 of 209 unseen episodes, never in 71; measured by the verifier) and then gives it no test where it is worst, no model candidate, no fallback and no clear question; it also treats the exam as the unseen line only, although the provided sheets are graded too and three of them fail today. (2) The calendar: the robot hours written down (17.5 to 21.5) are about half of what the steps need (30 to 48, calculated from measured times), the first frozen system comes seven days before robot work ends, and session 2 spends 24 of its 36 runs on sheets the model trained on. A first session with the models trained without each sheet needs no print and no new tool; it should be driven this week and followed by a frozen version 1. (3) Questions and decisions are out of date and out of order: at 22:02 on 11.11.26, after the plan was written (20:47), Emranul reported answers to four of the nine questions (file D:/AI_Project/claude_memory/project-exam-rules-reported.md); the decision on the exam lay-out rule, which settles how much 'any orientation' work the grade needs, is missing; Marshal can see none of the work. All numbers I use are in D:/AI_Project/.claude/worktrees/training-analysis/06_deliverables/research_notes/Model and training/evidence/robot_test_2026-11-11/plan_review/exam_and_schedule/ (timing_checks, rule_and_budget_checks, stall_rule_at_asked_pace and second_pass_checks, each a .py with its .out.txt; the first three are the earlier attempt's, rerun by me with the same output).

**The checker's overall verdict.** Most of the critique stands. I tested all 15 objections against the files: 9 stand (P1, P3, P5, P8, P10, P11, P12, P13, P14), 6 stand in part (P2, P4, P6, P7, P9, P15), none falls. Paths below are short for W = D:/AI_Project/.claude/worktrees/training-analysis/ (STATUS.md, COLLABORATION.md, HOW_WE_WORK.md, 02_code/, 05_meetings/) and E = W + "06_deliverables/research_notes/Model and training/evidence/robot_test_2026-11-11/". I reran the critic's timing and stall-rule scripts (same output) and recomputed its chances and hour sums; my files are in D:/AI_Project/.claude/worktrees/training-analysis/06_deliverables/research_notes/Model and training/evidence/robot_test_2026-11-11/plan_review/exam_and_schedule/check/ (rerun_*.out.txt, check_recording_times.py, check_rules_and_hours.py, each with its .out.txt). Four corrections matter. (1) The critique is itself out of date [measured]: D:/AI_Project/claude_memory/project-exam-rules-reported.md was changed at 22:22 and again at 22:55 on 11.11.26. It now says the first four answers are the professor's own words and adds four more (the robot must stop by itself; the team lays the unseen sheet and sets the start; one try per track; unseen tracks are seen only on the day), plus Emranul's own priority: finishing first, speed later. So the critic's first decision and three of its seven questions are answered, and its stop objection (P1) weighs more than it said. (2) One fix breaks an instruction [measured]: "commit now (needs no permission)" goes against W/STATUS.md lines 117 and 145, where nothing is committed without Emranul's go. (3) One fix goes against the verified results [measured in E/check_results_complete.md]: deleting block A removes the plain shorter fixed step that the step-length verifier calls a necessary control; the better fix keeps block A and drives it with the model trained without sheet 03. (4) Some sizes are off: 8.5 to 13 hours for the new shapes is nearer 6.5 to 10.5 [calculated; the 10.6 minutes come from the first recording day]; commits since 1.11 are 103 to 20, not 123 to 20 [measured]; and the critic's own calendar needs about 30 to 44 robot hours, as many as the plan, so it gains order and an early frozen version, not hours [calculated]. The direction is right throughout: drive the exam case this week with the models trained without each sheet, give the stop on an unseen line a test, a candidate and a fallback, freeze a first version early, put the lay-out rule to both students, and shorten the message to the professor.

### Objections

**P1** (high). Where: Section 2.2 cause 5 (lines 74 to 78); step 2 block B (line 164); step 3 row 'Line ends' (line 198); step 4 candidates (lines 208 to 211); section 8 second bullet (lines 281 to 283); section 9

- Problem: The plan says itself that on an unseen line 'does it stop at all' is a bigger risk than '5 mm short', and then leaves it without an answer. No block tests the stop where it is worst. No model candidate aims at it. The planned recording moves it by a few points only. Section 9 has no row for it. The one question that decides how serious it is sits as one of seven parts of question 3.
- Evidence: Measured by the verifier (stop_and_centring/verify/check_early_and_never_stops.out.txt): model E on sheets it never trained on stops in the right place in 106 of 209 episodes, never in 71, too early in 32; second training run 109, 51, 49. Right stops by sheet: 01 80 %, 03 76 %, 04 67 %, 06 32 %, 02 27 %, 05 21 %. Block B drives only sheet 03 as an unseen provided sheet, the second best. Calculated (exam_and_schedule/second_pass_checks.out.txt and rule_and_budget_checks.out.txt, from the fit of report 8.2, +8.7 points per doubling of stop pictures; frozen features, an extrapolation): 10 to 30 line-end episodes add +0.5 to +1.4 points; all of step 3 adds +2.5 to +4.4 points (106 becomes about 111 to 115 of 209); a doubling needs 259 more line ends, about 10 robot hours of near-end starts. Measured (report 4.5): three stop designs were never run end to end (stop trained on every picture within 2 cm of the end; stop only when the stop chance is high and the asked step short; hard examples); none is in step 4. Measured (report 4.5): 34 of 89 episodes with the line end 5 cm or more from the paper edge never stopped, against 16 of 120 nearer ends; the cause was not separated.
- The critic's fix: (1) In the first session drive sheets 02, 05 and 06 with the models trained without them (they exist: 04_runs/gpu_ki014/2026-11-10_resnet18_chunk_stop_weight_25/checkpoints/without_path_02.pt, _05.pt, _06.pt), 3 runs each, and write down for every run: reached the end yes or no; stopped there, stopped early, or never (Esc when the puck is 5 cm past the end or leaves the paper). (2) Add to step 4, before the other candidates: 'Stop candidates, each trained twice, judged on whole-episode stops on shapes not trained on, kept at 15 or more extra right stops of 209 without more early stops (report 9.1 item 8): the stop trained on every picture within 2 cm of the end, used at a high threshold; stop only when the stop chance passes and the asked step is short (no retraining); the team of five.' (3) Add to section 9: 'The robot does not stop, or stops early, on an unseen line | stop candidates first; near-end starts on every new track (2.4 minutes each, report 8.4); one development track with its end near the paper edge and one far from it: if the far end stops less often, the lay-out rule starts from the end whose far end is near the edge.' (4) Make 'Must the robot stop by itself?' the first question to the professor, alone, in one line.
- Check: **stands**. Checked the plan (lines 74 to 78, 164, 198, 208 to 211, 281 to 283, section 9): it names 'does it stop at all' as the bigger risk and gives it no test on the worst sheets, no model candidate and no risk row; section 9 has a row for 'stops short' only [measured]. E/stop_and_centring/verify/check_early_and_never_stops.out.txt gives 106 right, 71 never, 32 early of 209 for model E and 109, 51, 49 for the second run; by sheet 16 of 20, 12 of 45, 34 of 45, 30 of 45, 6 of 29, 8 of 25 [measured]. Report 4.5 lists the three stop designs never run end to end and the 34 of 89 against 16 of 120 [measured]. The gain of +0.5 to +4.4 points is right arithmetic on the report's fit of +8.7 points per doubling (section 8.2); it is an extrapolation from frozen features [calculated]. The objection weighs more now: by the memory file project-exam-rules-reported.md (changed 22:22 on 11.11.26) Emranul reports that the robot must stop by itself and that there is one try per track [measured]. Two parts of the fix do not hold. Part 4 (ask the professor whether the robot must stop by itself) is answered as reported. 'The team of five' is no stop candidate: 95 right stops of 209 against 103 and 105 for model E (checklist of 11.11, lines 83 and 86) [measured]. And a full run on unseen sheets 05 and 06 will probably lose the line in the loop before the end, so it shows nothing about the stop [guess, from the verified loop findings].
- The checker's fix: (1) In session 2a add a stop block that does not depend on getting through the track: near-end starts (tip 6 to 8 cm before the line's end, as in the 42 recorded near-end episodes) on sheets 02, 05 and 06 with the models trained without them, sheet 03 as the control, 3 runs each; note 'stopped on the end, early or never' (Esc when the puck is 5 cm past the end) and take the ruler photo. About 12 short runs, 30 to 45 minutes [calculated from 2.4 minutes per near-end start, report 8.4]. (2) Step 4, stop candidates on ki-014 (minutes each, no robot time), each trained twice, judged on whole-episode stops on shapes not trained on: the stop trained on every picture within 2 cm of the end, used at a high threshold, kept by the report's own rule (9.1 item 8: about 15 more right stops of 209 than both baseline runs, no more early stops); the combined rule (stop chance and a short asked step) on the saved models, kept by 9.1 item 9, also tried with a lower threshold so that never-stops can turn into right stops. Not the team of five. (3) The section 9 row as the critic words it. (4) No question to the professor; one line in the confirmation message of P4, and the aim in section 3 written as 'reaches the end and stops there by itself, on the first run'.

**P2** (high). Where: Step 2, the table of blocks (lines 155 to 170); decision 6 (line 251)

- Problem: Session 2 spends two thirds of its runs on sheets the model was trained on, although the plan's own cause 5 is that the exam case was never driven. The driver's step rule, the main driver decision, is settled in block A on one bend of a training sheet, by a rule that will often give no answer; block B has no rule, so decision 6 has nothing to decide with. Block C answers a question whose answer changes nothing in the plan.
- Evidence: Calculated from the plan's table (second_pass_checks.out.txt): 12 of 36 runs are on an unseen line; 24 are model E on sheets 02, 03, 05 and 06, which it trained on. Block A (rule_and_budget_checks.out.txt, calculated): with true pass chances of 0.9 for the new setting and 0.33 for fixed 1.0 cm the rule takes the new setting on with chance 0.57; with 0.75 and 0.33, 0.44; fixed 1.0 cm passes 2 or more of 4 with chance 0.40 and then no setting may be taken on. The twist verifier (check_results_complete.md, Twist and heading, overall verdict): a model that never saw sheet 03 gives almost the same answers at that bend, 'so the next session can test the exam case with a fold checkpoint'. Block C: with the same true finish chance of 0.5 for both prints the rule says 'dashes' with chance 0.25 (calculated); model E trained on both shapes; solid and dashed prints of every new track and the dash augmentation are in steps 1, 3 and 4 whatever block C shows. Its two special prints must be traced from photos: the six course sheets exist only as JPEG (01_course/path_sheets/, measured). The checklist of 11.11 already held the unseen-sheet test (phase 1a, lines 62 to 71, 12 runs) and it was not driven: all ten run folders name all-sheets models (measured).
- The critic's fix: Split session 2. Session 2a, this week, needs no print, no scorer and no new robot movement: model_mode.py already has --step-length predicted, --length-gain and --step-budget (lines 804 to 807, tested in test_model_mode.py; measured). Runs: the dry run of block 0; then sheets 02, 03, 05 and 06, each with the model trained without it, fixed 1.0 cm against the model's own length in pairs, 3 pairs a sheet, order alternating (24 runs; about 2.6 to 2.7 hours with a ruler photo at each stop; calculated in second_pass_checks.out.txt). Rule written before: in each pair the better run is the one whose puck got further along the line, or, if both reach the end, stopped nearer the end's centre; the model's own length is taken on if it is the better run in 9 or more of the 12 pairs. By chance alone that happens 7 times in 100; if it is truly better in 8 of 10 pairs the rule finds it 79 times in 100 (calculated). Otherwise fixed 1.0 cm stays and fixed 0.65 cm is tried in session 2b. Session 2b, after the prints: each development track once solid and once dashed with model E (this replaces block C and tests dashes on a shape no model knows), block D, the scorer. Delete block A.
- Check: **partly**. Right [measured from the plan's table]: 12 of 36 runs drive a line the model has not trained on; 18 are model E on sheets it trained on (blocks A and D, two repeats in C) and 6 are new prints of shapes it trained on (block C). Block B has no rule. The chances are right [calculated again, check/check_rules_and_hours.out.txt]: 0.57 and 0.44 for block A, 0.25 for block C, 0.073 and 0.795 for the pair rule. The twist verifier's sentence on the fold checkpoint is there (E/check_results_complete.md, Twist and heading, overall verdict). All ten run folders name all-sheets models, so phase 1a of the 11.11 checklist (lines 62 to 71) was not driven [measured, summary.json]. The driver has --step-length predicted, --length-gain and --step-budget (model_mode.py lines 804 to 807; test_model_mode.py line 71) [measured]. Too strong: 'will often give no answer' uses a pass chance of 0.33 for fixed 1.0 cm, which counts run 10 (model B); model E passed 0 of 2; at 0.1 to 0.2 the rule takes a good setting on in 0.78 to 0.90 [calculated]. 'Must be traced from photos': the sheets exist only as JPEG [measured], but centre lines from the course photos exist (dashed-line verifier, challenge 1). Wrong in the fix: deleting block A drops the fixed 0.65 cm step, which the step-length verifier calls necessary ('without this control a robot win of own length cannot be told from a win of shorter steps', challenge 3, missed point 4); freezing 'own length' would also bring the stop about 5 mm short (stop verifier, challenge 3). And session 2a would again start by hand with nothing logged; the verified results ask at least to print and log the offset from the recorded start pose (Unfamiliar pictures, recommendation 2 and its challenge; twist verifier, missed point 2).
- The checker's fix: Split session 2 as the critic says (2a this week, no prints), but keep the controls. 2a: block 0, the dry run of 1 to 3 mm steps. Block A with the model trained without sheet 03 instead of model E: fixed 1.0 cm, the model's own length, fixed 0.65 cm (the fixed length from the command line, already listed in step 1 item 2, with its test without the robot); 4 short runs each, alternating. Pass mark: the puck still touches the ink (under 17 mm) on two pictures in a row, because run 10 and one of five demonstrations fail the 12 mm wording (E/plan_review/robot_session/block_a_rule_on_the_three_runs.out.txt and block_a_rule_on_demonstrations.out.txt). Then full runs in pairs with the two best settings on sheets 02, 05 and 06, each with the model trained without it, 3 pairs a sheet; the critic's pair rule at 7 of 9 (by luck 0.09; found at a true 0.8 in 0.74) [calculated]; 'further along the line' is read from the logged pictures after the sitting. Then the stop block of P1. The start stays hand-placed (no new robot movement), but the driver prints and logs the offset from the recorded start pose, and the checklist says: place again above 1 cm or 3 degrees. Sheets on tape marks. About 3 hours, in two sittings [calculated from 3.9 minutes a run]. 2b after the prints: each development track solid and dashed (this replaces block C), block D as a labelled extra.

**P3** (high). Where: Section 5 calendar (lines 233 to 240); step 3 table (lines 192 to 199); steps 5 and 6; section 9 last row (line 299)

- Problem: The robot hours the plan writes down are about half of what its own steps need, five robot items have no hours at all, and the first frozen system comes on 13.12, seven days before robot work ends. Nothing that works is frozen early, so any slip (a late answer, an illness, a moved camera) lands on the correction rounds and the final test. As written it is not realistic for two students, of whom one drives the robot; it becomes realistic with the cuts below.
- Evidence: The plan's own hours: 3 + 0.5 + (6 + 3 + 1 to 3) + (4 to 6) = 17.5 to 21.5; session 3, the paired test, the final test, the light block and the rehearsal have none. Calculated item by item from measured unit times (rule_and_budget_checks.out.txt): 30 to 48 hours, 33 to 54 with a second correction round, that is 5.5 to 8.6 hours in each of the 5.6 weeks from 12.11 to 20.12. Report 8.4 gave 31 to 46 hours for much the same programme. Measured unit times (timing_checks.out.txt): 3.93 minutes a model run start to start on 11.11; 8.13 minutes per kept demonstration over all sittings of 6 to 9.11 (28.5 hours for 210). The 5.8 minutes the plan uses is the median gap inside one sheet; it leaves out breaks, discarded episodes (29 of 239 folders) and lay-out changes. The first five episodes on a sheet took 10.6 minutes on average, and each of the 4 episodes on a new shape is such an episode. So '48 episodes, about 6 hours' is about 8.5 to 13 hours; the orientation verifier's own formula (5.8 minutes plus a guessed 10 minutes a lay-out) gives 8.6. Session 2: 3.5 to 4.7 hours against 'about three'. Step 1: 3 to 6 days by the analysts' own efforts against 'two to three' (second_pass_checks.out.txt; two of the six items are my guesses). The correction tool does not exist: model_mode.py has no take-over and record_episodes.py no model part (grep, measured); the plan gives it no build time and the word 'pilot' does not occur in it. 193 of the 210 kept episodes are Emranul's, 17 Marshal's (measured). For feasibility: 28.5 hours were recorded in the four days 6 to 9.11 (measured).
- The critic's fix: Replace section 5 by a calendar with an early frozen version and a written cut list. By 15.11: session 2a (P2). **P10** (medium). Where: Step 1 item 2, the stall rule (lines 142 to 144)

- Problem: The new stall rule is fitted to one run of 11.11 (run 6) and was checked only at the recorded pace of about 1 cm a picture. At the pace of the step rule the plan wants to test, it would end runs that are not stalled. A stall rule can only save time on a run that is already lost; a wrong firing loses a run that could have finished.
- Evidence: Calculated (exam_and_schedule/stall_rule_at_asked_pace.out.txt; a paper check: the answers of the models that never saw each sheet, laid end to end on the demonstrators' pictures, not a robot run): the plan's rule (15 pictures, 4 cm, 15 degrees) fires in 0 of 209 episodes at the recorded steps but in 19 of 209 (9 %) when the tip moves the asked length, 8 of them within 5 cm of the end and 11 further away. The driver's present rule (30 pictures, 3 cm, 15 degrees; config.py MODEL_MODE_PARAMETERS) fires in 0 of 209 at every pace. Another critic's own script gives 17 of 209 (plan_review/robot_session/stall_rule_with_short_steps.out.txt). Asked length in the last 5 cm before the end: median 0.51 cm, 16 % under 0.3 cm (measured from step_length/fold_outputs_E_recipe.npz).
- The critic's fix: Replace the stall-rule clause by: 'the stall rule stays as it is in config.py (30 pictures, 3 cm, 15 degrees); any other rule is first checked on the unseen-sheet answers at the pace of the step rule in use and must fire in none of the 209 episodes; Esc stays the way to end a run that goes back and forth.'
- Check: **stands**. I reran stall_rule_at_asked_pace.py: the output is identical (check/rerun_stall_rule_at_asked_pace.out.txt) [measured]. The plan's rule (15 pictures, 4 cm, 15 degrees) fires in 0 of 209 unseen-sheet episodes at the recorded pace and in 19 of 209 when the tip moves the asked length, 11 of them more than 5 cm before the end. The driver's present rule (30 pictures, 3 cm, 15 degrees; 02_code/config.py lines 1908 to 1910) fires in none at any pace. A second critic's script with another method gives 17 of 209 for model E and 29 of 209 for model A (E/plan_review/robot_session/stall_rule_with_short_steps.out.txt) [measured]. It is a paper check on the demonstrators' pictures, as the critic says. Two notes: the present rule would not have ended run 6 as driven (same folder, stall_rule_on_the_ten_runs.out.txt), so Esc stays the tool against back and forth; and with one try per track (Emranul's report) a wrong firing loses the track, while a stall rule can never win one.
- The checker's fix: The critic's wording as it is, plus one line: 'at the exam the stall rule stays at this loose setting; it only saves time on a run that is already lost'.

**P11** (medium). Where: Step 1 item 4 (lines 149 to 151); step 3 table, first row (line 196); step 5 (lines 218 to 222); step 6 (line 227)

The drawing rule is committed first; then Marshal draws the sealed set on his laptop with a seed he keeps, prints it and keeps it in an envelope; its files stay out of git until the final test. Dashed prints follow sheet 06's dashes; report 8.4 item 4 asked for another rhythm. **P12** (medium). Where: Step 4 (lines 206 to 214)

- Problem: The rule that chooses between models cannot see what is aimed for. A model is kept on '5 points of path within 45 degrees', measured against demonstrations that are themselves off-centre and often do not cover the end; the stop is 'reported next to' the score but decides nothing. The candidate list has no candidate for the stop, the end or centring, but two research items that change the driver which step 5 wants frozen.
- Evidence: Plan lines 71 to 73: the puck within 7 mm of the line's middle in 68 % of pushed pictures, ends covered in 52 to 60 %, 'A model that copies cannot centre better than what it copies'. The stop-and-centring analyst, recommendation 5, risk: 'offline direction scores will not show a gain in centring, only robot runs with the new numbers will'. Candidates (lines 208 to 211): the baseline; dashes cut in with a wider shift and coloured patches; a step target that separates 'which way' from 'how far' (the analyst's effort: 1 to 2 days of code per recipe, after a one-hour probe); the previous picture (report 4.5: no gain for the stop). Step 5 freezes the driver in session 3 (line 219).
- The critic's fix: Name two judges. Without the robot, on shapes not trained on, each candidate trained twice: path within 45 by the 5-point rule AND whole-episode right stops (no fewer than the baseline; 15 more of 209 for a stop candidate). On the robot, for anything about the end, centring or orientation: two versions driven one after the other from the same start on development tracks, scored from the pictures (end within 7 mm; share of pictures within 7 mm). Candidates in this order, the list closed before version 2 is frozen: the baseline on the new data; a stop candidate (P1); dashes cut in with the wider shift and the patches; stop pictures only from covered ends as a robot-judged variant (the verifier warns that it halves the stop examples, so it must not lower the right stops). The 'which way and how far' target only if its one-hour probe on the laptop shows a gain. Drop the previous picture.
- Check: **stands**. [measured] Step 4 keeps a candidate on '5 points of path within 45 degrees'; the stop is 'reported next to every score' (line 153) and decides nothing; the list has no candidate for the stop, the end or centring. Plan lines 70 to 73 give the demonstrations' own centring (68 % of pushed pictures within 7 mm; ends covered in 52 to 60 %). The stop-and-centring analyst's recommendation 5 names the risk as quoted ('offline direction scores will not show a gain in centring'). The step-length analyst gives 'about an hour' for the probe and '1 to 2 days of code' per recipe (recommendation 6). Report 5.4 and 4.5 measured no gain from the previous picture. One sub-claim is weak: the two research items do not collide with the driver freeze, because step 4 comes before session 3 in the plan's own order; they only arrive late. And from P1: the team of five is no stop candidate (95 right stops against 103 and 105).
- The checker's fix: Two judges, as the critic says. Without the robot, on shapes not trained on, each candidate trained twice: path within 45 by the 5-point rule and whole-episode stops (no fewer right stops than the baseline). On the robot, for the end, centring and the stop: version against version from the same start on ten or more development shapes, one pair each, scored from the pictures and the ruler photos; at most one such comparison besides version 2 against version 1, because each costs 1.7 to 4.8 hours (report 7.4). Candidates, the list closed before version 2 is frozen: the baseline on the new data; the two stop candidates of P1; dashes cut in with the wider shift and the patches; stop pictures only from covered ends as a robot-judged variant that must not lower the right stops. The 'which way and how far' target only if its one-hour probe shows a gain. No previous picture.

**P13** (medium). Where: Step 1 item 5 (lines 152 to 153); step 3 rule 4 (lines 186 to 187); section 2.1, run 7 (line 43)

- Problem: A fixed test ties every later model to one run on a training sheet. 'A retrained model must still ask for the walk on dashed copies of run 7's pictures' demands a manoeuvre that the plan itself calls probably learnt by heart, and rule 4 of step 3 leaves open whether the walk stays the way through a loop at all. If the hand trial chooses the other way, this check fails every good model.
- Evidence: Plan line 43: run 7's success 'is probably learnt by heart' (a model that never saw sheet 05 answers on the same pictures with a correlation of 0.11). Lines 186 to 187: 'either the walk round the puck as recorded, or unwinding the forearm early on a straight stretch'. Dashed-line recommendation 5 in check_results_complete.md: if a new loop style is adopted, 13 to 25 loop episodes are recorded again, 2 to 3 hours; the plan's recording table has no line for it.
- The critic's fix: Replace the check by one that does not depend on the manoeuvre: 'On dashed copies of the same pictures a model's twist answers may differ from its answers on the originals by no more than the control repaint does (12 %). Reported for every model, on run 7's pictures and on demonstration pictures of shapes it did not train on.' Add to the table of step 3: 'The loop sheets again in the new style, only if the hand trial changes the way through a loop | 13 to 25 episodes | 2 to 3 hours.'
- Check: **stands**. [measured] Plan line 43 calls run 7's success 'probably learnt by heart' (correlation 0.11 with a model that never saw sheet 05). Lines 152 to 153 make 'still asks for the walk on dashed copies of run 7's pictures' a check for every retrained model. Lines 186 to 187 leave open whether the walk stays the way through a loop. So a good model trained in the other loop style would fail the check. The check came from the dashed-line verifier (challenge 6) as a test of the dashes, not of the walk; the control repaint changes the twist by 12 % (plan line 82). The dashed-line analyst's recommendation 5 gives 2 to 3 hours for 13 to 25 episodes if a new loop style is adopted; that figure is for sheet 06 alone, and the plan's recording table has no line for it.
- The checker's fix: The critic's replacement, as a report and not as a gate: for every model, the share of twist lost on dashed copies against the same model on the originals, beside the control repaint (12 %; today 62 to 70 % for model E), on run 7's pictures and on demonstration pictures of shapes the model did not train on. Add the conditional row to the table of step 3: 'the loop sheets again in the new style, only if the hand trial changes the way through a loop | 13 to 25 episodes per loop sheet | 2 to 3 hours each', with the rule that the old loop episodes of that sheet are then left out of training.

### Missed by the critic

1. One try per track changes how everything should be tested, and neither the plan nor the critique draws that. Measured: the memory file D:/AI_Project/claude_memory/project-exam-rules-reported.md (changed 22:22 on 11.11.26) records one try per track, unseen tracks seen only on the day, and the team laying the sheet. The plan and the critique still test with 2 to 3 runs on few tracks. Report 7.5: 30 runs on 3 tracks count like 12.8 independent runs, on 10 tracks like 23.1 [calculated there from a guess]. Fix: write 'the first run on a track never driven before' into section 3 and step 6; final test as twenty sealed tracks x 1 plus six sheets x 3; make it the rehearsal too: an unseen print, laid by the rule against a clock, one run.
2. A refused step ends the run, and nobody has said what happens then. Measured: 02_code/model_mode.py ends a run at the first refused step (lines 414 and 421); the checklist of 11.11 says so (line 112); report 1.1 row 4, 'what the robot does after a refused step', is still 'none proposed yet'; the word does not occur in the plan. With the wrist held the lean limit ends a run at about 47.6 cm of reach (orientation verifier, challenge 3) [calculated there]. With one try that is a lost track; how often an unseen line gets there is a guess. Fix: a row in section 9 and a decision card for Emranul (end the run, try a shorter step, or hold); the reach line in the lay-out rule (P5); in session 2a one dry run without the puck to the far end of a sheet to see where the driver refuses.
3. The exam can be driven from one laptop by one person. Measured: model_mode.py imports msvcrt (line 29; used for Esc in lines 189 to 190), a Windows-only part of Python, and Marshal works on a MacBook (COLLABORATION.md line 3), so the driver does not start there as written; the model files and the data are outside git; the branch is not on the remote; 193 of 210 demonstrations and all ten runs are Emranul's. The critique asks for a backup and a share for Marshal, not for a second way to run the exam. Fix: when version 1 is frozen, copy its code, model file and package list to a second disk and start it once on a second machine (or make the Esc reader work on macOS, a few lines with a test without the robot); Marshal drives one block of session 2a or 2b with Emranul beside him; the exam-day sheet names who runs the program and who lays the sheet.
4. The repository is public and the grade is ranked against other groups. Measured: COLLABORATION.md line 8 says '(public)'; the Moodle snapshot lists seven groups on Project 6; the finishing time is ranked against the other groups (professor, 26.10). The push that the critique puts first would publish the plan, the analysis of the failures, the driver, the track generator with its seed, and pool details in a memory file. Whether another group reads it is a guess. Fix: one question to both students before the push: keep the repository public, or make it private until 11.1.27 (Marshal owns it); the sealed tracks and their seed never go in.
5. Six development shapes carry every choice in the critique's own fixes (the step rule, version 2 against version 1, each candidate). That is the same trap as six course sheets: after several choices the versions are fitted to those six [judgement], and three runs on six shapes tell less than one pair on each of twelve (report 7.5). Report 7.4 asked for sets of at least ten [measured].
